# Physics channels handled by LiPolGen — approach, references, implementing functions

LiPolGen generates doubly polarized deep-inelastic scattering of a polarized
electron beam on a polarized ⁶Li or ⁷Li ion beam (with d, ³He and p available as
control species) at EIC energies, in three fidelity tiers — **T0** (electron,
struck object and one hadronic pseudo-particle X), **T1** (the struck cluster
resolved into a struck nucleon plus on-shell partner spectators) and **T2** (the
PYTHIA 8 hadronic final state) — across five `PipelineChannel` values:
`Inclusive`, `TaggedLi6Alpha` (⁶Li(e,e′α)X, DIS on the embedded deuteron),
`TaggedLi7Alpha` (⁷Li(e,e′α)X, DIS on the quasi-free triton), `TaggedDeuteronP`
(d(e,e′p)X, the Cosyn–Weiss tagged limit used as a control) and `CoherentLi6`
(e + ⁶Li → e′ + X + ⁶Li g.s.). Almost every physics ingredient beyond the
default toy backends is **opt-in**: the exact finite-γ tensor kernel, the ANL VMC
cluster wave functions, the Ciofi degli Atti–Simula triton spectral function, the
Glauber spectator-FSI weight, the tensor-sector radiative-correction weight
family, the α–d convolution backend for b₁(⁶Li), the LHAPDF structure-function
backends, the polarized-EMC transfer curves and the PYTHIA hadronizer are all
switched off or replaced by a toy unless a run asks for them. Two subsystems are
documented here but are **not part of event generation at all**: the polarized ⁶Li
nucleon-configuration sampler of §10, which writes a file for an external
dipole-model code, and the polarized-EMC transfer curves of §3. This document
says, per subsystem, what is modelled, how, with what defaults and bands, on
whose authority, and by which function.

**Every symbol below is given as a repo-relative `file:line` valid at the
commit that last touched this file (`git log -1 -- docs/PHYSICS_CHANNELS.md`)
and is checked mechanically by `validation/check_physics_channels_links.py`,
STRICT since 2026-09-04: a named `path:line` must land on the line that
DECLARES the name, a range must still hash to its recorded fingerprint, and a
bare `File.cc:line` (PYTHIA upstream) is read from the tree `env.sh` names or
skipped by name.  The tally moves with every phase, so read it off the gate's
own printed line rather than from here — it was **1205 refs, 97 ranges, 7
external, 0 broken, 6 allow-listed** when this sentence was last measured
(2026-09-05, at the end of the run's close-out; it read 1172 earlier the same
day, before the close-out added 33 citations, and "1094 refs, 6 external"
before that, which was the C9-era count and stale from phase D on).** Where the repository
carries no citation for a number, this document says so instead of inventing one.

---

## 0. Channel overview

| channel | final state | what is polarized | tiers | opt-in models | entry symbols |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Inclusive` | e′ + struck nucleon (p/n drawn ∝ Z·F₂ᵖ : N·F₂ⁿ) + X (pdg 92) | electron helicity λ_e; ion vector P_z and rank-2 P_zz. Rank-2/Δ slots are filled by default only for a **spin-1** ion (⁶Li *and* d); a ⁷Li run gets b₁ = b₂ = Δ ≡ 0 | T0 only (no struck cluster to resolve; the (A−1) remnant is not written) | `tensor_gamma`, `target_mass`, `g2_mode`/`g2_scale`, `with_perp`, a custom kernel — none of them on the CLI; **on the CLI**: `--b1-model cdks\|li6-convolution` with the mandatory `--b1-band-scale` 0/1/2 (and, on `li6-convolution` only, `--b1-alpha-d-dwave-weight`), both needing `--x-max 0.95` (⁶Li only, §3), `--unpol-sf`/`--pol-sf` (§3), and `--rc tensor-band` — the only channel where BOTH the RC band and the radiative tails apply (§8) | `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3396` `make_inclusive`; `src/core/xsec.cpp:282` `amplitudes` |
| `TaggedLi6Alpha` — ⁶Li(e,e′α)X | e′ + on-shell α spectator + (T1) struck nucleon + partner nucleon + X | λ_e; the ⁶Li J=1 fill, transferred to the embedded deuteron through the α–d S+D wave (`vector_dilution`, `tensor_dilution`); the DIS target is the deuteron | T0 / **T1 (default)** | `--cluster-wave vmc`, `--fsi`, `--inclusive-b1`, `--p-d`, `--cluster-beta`, `--unpol-sf`/`--pol-sf` (§3), `--rc tensor-band` (**band only** — `rc_tail` ≡ 1 on a tagged channel, §8) | `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3406` `make_tagged`; `src/core/tagged.cpp:243` `li6_alpha_channel` |
| `TaggedLi7Alpha` — ⁷Li(e,e′α)X | e′ + on-shell α spectator + (T1) struck nucleon + d or (nn)/(pn) partners + X | λ_e; the ⁷Li J=3/2 fill through the pure L=1 α–t wave; the DIS target is the triton (P_p=+0.86, P_n=−0.028) | T0 / **T1 (default)** | `--cluster-wave vmc`, `--triton-sf ciofi-simula`, `--fsi`, `--unpol-sf`/`--pol-sf` (§3); `--rc tensor-band` (band only) — but on ⁷Li the only plan this CLI builds is `helicity-flip`, whose default `--pe 0.7` makes the `RcModel` constructor throw, so the band is reached on ⁷Li only as `--plan helicity-flip --pe 0` (§8) | `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3406` `make_tagged`; `src/core/tagged.cpp:278` `li7_alpha_channel` |
| `TaggedDeuteronP` — d(e,e′p)X | e′ + on-shell proton spectator + struck neutron + X | λ_e; the deuteron J=1 fill through the p–n S+D wave; the DIS target is a free neutron (P_p=0, P_n=1) | T0 / **T1 (default, a pure relabel — the cluster *is* the nucleon)** | `--fsi` (both Glauber variants coincide here), `--cluster-beta`, `--unpol-sf`/`--pol-sf` (§3 — this is the channel where the toy g₁ⁿ's WRONG SIGN over 0.25 < x < 0.6 bites hardest, the DIS target being a free neutron; the PAIR `--unpol-sf ct18nlo\|mstw --pol-sf nnpdfpol` needs **`--x-max 0.95`** under `--plan helicity-flip` at `--pe` ≠ 0, because the neutron A₁ = g₁/F₁ reaches 4.40579 resp. 4.77729 in the window's top cell and 1 + w_avg goes negative — refused by name, §3), `--rc tensor-band` (band only, §8) | `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3406` `make_tagged`; `src/core/tagged.cpp:307` `deuteron_channel` |
| `CoherentLi6` — e + ⁶Li → e′ + X + ⁶Li g.s. | e′ + intact on-shell ⁶Li recoil + Pomeron (documentation) + X | λ_e is carried as a label only: the coherent **rate is spin-independent**; the tensor signal is the cos 2(φ_t − φ_S) modulation of the recoil azimuth driven by P_zz | T0, plus T2 on a PYTHIA Pomeron beam | `--coherent-t2`, `--pom-set`, `--pom-rescale`, `--coherent-f0/-slope-b/-amp`, `--unpol-sf` (§3 — the channel owns no structure function and inherits it through the inclusive cell cross sections: ×0.705841 on the shipped ⁶Li `ct18nlo` run). **`--pol-sf` does NOT reach this channel** — the rate above is spin-independent and no g₁ is evaluated anywhere in the run (measured: σ, σ per category and all 47 generated array columns are bit-identical between `toy` and `nnpdfpol`), so it is *accepted and labelled* rather than refused: `meta["pol_sf"]` carries `not read on channel coherent-6Li` instead of a backend name and `meta["pol_sf_reach"]` the reason (`pol_sf_is_read(config, plan)` — and since 2026-09-05 that predicate carries a SECOND axis, the run plan, so the same label appears on every channel under an unpolarised-beam fill; see the knob-provenance row in §3). `--rc` is accepted and then **does nothing**: `RcModel::applies()` is false here and both weights are exactly 1.0, with the reason printed (§8) — but since 2026-09-05 a non-default rc SUB-knob is **refused** here rather than recorded, because it would name a systematic as priced that was never computed (the knob-provenance criterion in §3) — except that the constructor's unpolarised-beam check runs *before* the channel rule, so `--plan helicity-flip` at `--pe` ≠ 0 still throws here. The offline ⁶Li configuration sampler of §10 is the input a real polarized calculation for this channel would need | `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3540` `make_coherent`; `src/core/coherent.cpp:369` `sample` |

Every channel closes an exact four-momentum identity with one pseudo-particle X
(`Role::HadronicX`, pdg 92): the inclusive channel closes the **per-nucleon**
balance k + p_N = k′ + X; the three tagged channels close the **whole-nucleus**
balance k + P_ion = k′ + p_spec + Σp_partner + X; the coherent channel closes
k + P_ion = k′ + P_recoil + X. X is required to be timelike — a hard throw, never
a clip (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:3368` `add_hadronic_x`).

---

## 1. Beam and target: spin density matrices, effective polarizations, beam energies

The library treats any ion beam as an incoherent mixture of |J m⟩ populations
p_m (m = +J … −J, summing to 1) along a quantization axis n(θ_S, φ_S). The
lab-frame density matrix is ρ_lab = U diag(p_m) U†, with
U_{m′m} = D^J_{m′m}(φ_S, θ_S, 0) = exp(−i m′ φ_S) d^J_{m′m}(θ_S). Observables are
traces against operators built in the same m = +J … −J ordering: the vector
polarization P = ⟨J⟩/J, the rank-2 moment (P_zz = ⟨3J_z²−2⟩ for J=1;
T = ⟨3J_z²−J(J+1)⟩/3 for J=3/2) and the rank-3 moment
O = ⟨J_z³ − (41/20) J_z⟩/(3/10), which exists only at J=3/2.

For ⁶Li the whole-nucleus effective polarization is the CLUSTER-PICTURE product
`LI6_CLUSTER_POLARIZATION` = (1 − 1.5·P_D(α–d))·(1 − 1.5·P_D(d)) =
0.86995 × 0.9325 = 0.81123, split a third each into `eff_pol_p` and `eff_pol_n`
so that Z·P_p = N·P_n = 0.81123 in g₁A = Z·P_p·g₁ᵖ + N·P_n·g₁ⁿ.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| General spin-J density matrix, Wigner-D and multipole machinery | ρ_lab from populations + axis; Clebsch–Gordan by the exact Racah sum; small-d by direct summation; J_x,J_y,J_z built from the raising operator J⁺ and its adjoint J⁻ (J_x=(J⁺+J⁻)/2, J_y=−i(J⁺−J⁻)/2, J_z diagonal); a general irreducible tensor ⟨j m′\|T_kq\|j m⟩ = ⟨j m; k q\|j m′⟩√((2k+1)/(2j+1)). Generic machinery: no default population, it is driven by whatever `--pz`/`--pzz` or run plan supplies. Axis angles come from `HelicityFlipOptions::theta_s/phi_s` (`include/lipolgen/bookkeeping.hpp:157-158`) and the `tensor_thirds_plan` arguments (`include/lipolgen/bookkeeping.hpp:175-176`) | [HJM89]; Varshalovich conventions [Varsh] | `include/lipolgen/spin.hpp:60` `m_values`; `src/core/spin.cpp:95` `clebsch_gordan`; `src/core/spin.cpp:116` `wigner_d`; `src/core/spin.cpp:146` `rotation_matrix`; `src/core/spin.cpp:158` `angular_momentum_ops`; `src/core/spin.cpp:184` `multipole_operator`; `src/core/spin.cpp:199` `rho_from_populations`; `src/core/spin.cpp:219` `vector_polarization`; `include/lipolgen/spin.hpp:128` `SpinDensity` |

Three reference points (≈5×41, 10×100, 18×275) are built from AME2020 nuclear masses with round-half-even rounding to 0.1 GeV/u — never rounded literals. The code reproduces d = 137.5 and ³He = 183 GeV/u; the Au = 110 GeV/u number is only quoted in the `include/lipolgen/beams.hpp:13` header comment (Au is not a supported species). (19d) (the rank-3 partner function); [JM89] (the arbitrary-spin basis the note maps onto); [Cosyn25] (the spin-1 rank-2 block reused unchanged). ## 2. Inclusive DIS kernel: F₁/F₂/R, g₁/g₂, A∥/A⊥, the tensor sector b₁…b₄ and Δ

The unpolarized doubly-differential rate is

    d²σ/dx dQ² = (4πα²/(x Q⁴)) [ (1 − y + y²/2) F₂ − (y²/2) F_L ] ,

converted to pb by `GEV2_TO_PB`; the φ-averaged bracket every polarized term is
divided by is D_φ = F₁ + (1−y)/(x y²)·F₂. On top of it the kernel builds the
azimuthal density

    W(φ′) = 1 + w_avg + a₁ cos φ′ + a₂ cos 2φ′ ,   φ′ = φ − φ_S ,

with the vector-longitudinal term entering as
w_avg += λ_e·P_e·(m/J)·cos θ_S·A∥ (evaluated only when λ_e·P_e ≠ 0 and m ≠ 0 —
a pure projection m carries vector polarization m/J, so an m = 0 state has no
vector-L term for any spin, `src/core/xsec.cpp:266-270`), the rank-2 term as
w_avg += TENSOR_LL_SIGN·Q_NN·½(3cos²θ_S−1)·K/D_φ with K = b₁ + (1−y)/(xy²)·b₂,
and the double-helicity-flip term as
a₂ += −(1−y)/y²·c_eff·sin²θ_S·Δ/D_φ. The alignment geometry is one tensor for
any spin, t_ij = (Q_NN/2)(3n_i n_j − δ_ij) with Q_NN(m) = [3m² − J(J+1)]/3
(Cosyn Eq. 9), reducing to the HJM c_m = 3m² − 2 digit-for-digit at J = 1.

## 3. Structure-function backends: toy SFs, LHAPDF, b₁ camps, Δ models, EMC curves

Every physics input in this layer is an interface with a **toy implementation
always available** and a table/LHAPDF implementation optional. The
production defaults — `ToyF2`, `ToyG1`, `r_sigma_lt` — are explicitly
placeholders of the right magnitude, not fits. **Four CLI flags select a
backend** (three until 2026-09-06), and no more: the b₁ camp of the inclusive rank-2 slot
(`--b1-model miller|cdks|li6-convolution`, ⁶Li and the inclusive channel only,
with a mandatory band — the three rows that follow the `TensorSF` row), and,
since 2026-09-04, the unpolarised and polarised structure-function backends
(`--unpol-sf toy|mstw|ct18nlo` and `--pol-sf toy|nnpdfpol`, both defaulting to
the toy and then bit for bit — the two rows after the toy-SF rows below).
**Those two do not have the same reach.** `--unpol-sf` reaches **every kernel
the pipeline builds**, on every channel and every plan. `--pol-sf` reaches the
inclusive and the tagged kernels **only where the fill also carries
`lam_e · P_e ≠ 0`**, and never the coherent channel, whose rate is
spin-independent: under `tensor-thirds`, the CLI's own default plan, it is
read on **no** channel. `Pipeline::knob_provenance` is the authority on both
(`pol_sf_is_read(config, plan)`), and it labels rather than refuses. **The
fourth, added 2026-09-06, is narrower than either**:
`--r-source unset|sigma-lt|r1998` (`RSource`,
`PipelineConfig::r_source`) is the ONE R = σ_L/σ_T hook threaded into
`Li6ConvolutionOptions::r_func` **and** `InclusiveKernel::Options::r_func`
together, so the ⁶Li tensor weight K/D_φ has one R in its numerator and its
denominator. It is read on `--b1-model li6-convolution` and **refused
everywhere else** (`validate()`), its default `unset` installs neither hook
and is bit for bit, and `sigma-lt` — which names the value `resolve_r`'s null
branch already returns — is accepted, measured bit-identical and therefore
labelled `not-read`. It is registry row 3's option (iii), implemented opt-in
and priced, not taken: sharing `r1998` moves K/D_φ (and A_zz, −(2/3) of it)
by −3.8357 % / +26.3988 % / +3.3518 % at x = 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.30 (y = 0.5,
Q² = 2.5) against the numerator-only swap's −5.5148 % / +24.6099 % /
+2.6629 %, and it moves the unpolarised rate too (σ −0.684681 %), which no
other backend flag does — `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-06/phase_A_numbers.md`
§A1. Nothing else here — no other R hook, no EMC baseline, no Δ
camp — has a CLI or config-file key. On the **Inclusive** channel everything
else is injectable through the opaque `PipelineConfig::kernel` built from
`InclusiveKernel::Options`, and `validate()` refuses that together with a
non-Miller `--b1-model` or with either SF selector; on the **tagged** channels
`struck_cluster_kernel` exposes `inclusive_b1`, `delta_func` and (only since
2026-09-04) `f2_source`/`g1_model` — `PipelineConfig::kernel` does *not* reach
them, because `Pipeline` reads that field on the non-tagged branch alone, and
since 2026-09-05 `validate()` **refuses** a caller-supplied kernel on any
tagged channel rather than record `"caller-supplied kernel"` for a kernel that
never ran (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:1016`) — so
F₂ and g₁ there follow the two selectors while **EMC remains hard-locked to
`none` on every channel and R is hard-locked to `r_sigma_lt` on every channel
but one**: since 2026-09-06 `--r-source` reaches R on the inclusive ⁶Li
`li6-convolution` path alone, and `validate()` refuses it everywhere else.

The moment-constrained Δ solves A from A·∫ x F₁(x,Q²_ref) shape(x) dx = c_moment
(Interpretation A, default c_moment = `C_BAG` = −0.012) and then evaluates
Δ = dilution·A·α_s(Q²)·F₁·shape(x); Interpretation B drops the F₁ factor and
solves the same moment analytically through the Beta function. The shape is
x^α(1−x)^β normalized to peak 1, with variants low_x (0.3,4.0),
mid_x (0.7,3.0, the production default) and high_x (1.5,2.0).

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| Toy unpolarized F₂ᵖ/F₂ⁿ and the default R | sea = 0.20·x^(−λ)(1−x)⁷ with λ = 0.045·ln(max(Q²,1.1)/0.04); val = 1.05·x^0.55(1−x)³; F₂ᵖ = sea+val; F₂ⁿ = F₂ᵖ·clip(1−0.75x, 0.25, 1). Anchored **by eye** to F₂ᵖ(1e-3,10) ≈ 1.2, F₂ᵖ(0.1,10) ≈ 0.36, F₂ᵖ(0.4,10) ≈ 0.12 — "adequate for phase-space maps and factor-1.5 rate estimates ONLY". Program-wide default `UnpolSF` and, since 2026-09-04, only the DEFAULT of one — `--unpol-sf mstw|ct18nlo` replaces it in every kernel the pipeline builds (the row two below). Default R is the toy `r_sigma_lt` everywhere unless an `RFunc` override is supplied, and **the only CLI flag that supplies one is `--r-source`, on the ⁶Li `li6-convolution` path alone** (the `RSource` row below; refused everywhere else, so the sentence "no CLI flag supplies one" was true of the whole program until 2026-09-06 and is still true of every other path) | — (both explicitly labelled TOY in `include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:43-45` and `109-112`) | `include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:78` `UnpolSF`; `include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:114` `ToyF2`; `src/core/sf.cpp:102` `f2p`; `src/core/sf.cpp:114` `f2n`; `include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:46` `r_sigma_lt`; `include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:147` `dsigma_dx_dq2` |

Three surfaces read that one table and nothing else — the npz `meta["knob_provenance"]` block (plus the five keys that carry a label where the knob did not run: `pol_sf`, `rc_scope`, `coherent_t2`, `pom_set`, `pom_rescale`), the CLI's `KNOB PROVENANCE` banner block, and `python/tests/test_knob_provenance.py`, which rebuilds the (channel × plan × knob) matrix and asserts the table against the **output hash** of two small runs (moved → `read`; did not move → `not-read`; refused → `refused`) and fails on any row with no matrix entry and no excuse. It is a method on the **run** because three rules are properties of the run and not of the config: the rc reaches are `RcModel::applies()` / `tail_applies()` on the model this run built, the tagged breakup rows key on `Pipeline::tier()` (T0 on channels with no struck cluster whatever the config says), and `--x-max`'s coherent reach is decided against this run's own upper accepted-x edge — measured x = 0.1, above which no cell carries coherent rate (the largest such cell's centre is 0.0954992586). `KnobRunContext` carries the two things the core cannot see: the T2 bridge (the optional PYTHIA tier, recovered by the metadata writer from the hook actually bound) and what the caller asked the plan factory for. **The criterion, stated once in `KnobProvenance`'s header:** REFUSE when the value would name a *variation of a piece that did not run* — a scale, a band edge or a shape on a term the run computes as identically 1, because that claims a systematic was PRICED (`b1_band_scale` on miller, `rc_qe_tensor_scale` without the QE tail, every rc tail sub-knob on a tagged channel); LABEL when it names a *backend, an axis or a member of a family a channel-, plan- or set-scan sets uniformly* (`--pol-sf` on coherent AND under every unpolarised-beam plan, `--pzz-mode` under the three tensor plans — it was `--pzz` under helicity-flip until 2026-09-06, when `--pzz-mode typed` made that flag reachable there, `--pom-set` off coherent). | **`--pol-sf`** — the polarised backend of the inclusive and tagged kernels | Chooses `InclusiveKernel::Options::g1_model` and `StruckClusterOptions::g1_model`. **SCOPE — TWO axes, and the second is the shipped default.** g₁ enters through exactly one product, `lam_e · P_e · (m/J) · cos θ_S · A_∥` in `InclusiveKernel::amplitudes`, so the selector is read only where the CHANNEL evaluates g₁ **and** the FILL carries `lam_e · P_e ≠ 0`. It does not reach the **coherent channel** — that rate is spin-independent, nothing there evaluates g₁ — and it does not reach **any unpolarised-beam plan**: `tensor_thirds_plan`, `transverse_tensor_plan` and `tensor_flip_plan` build every category at `lam_e = 0, pe = 0`, and `tensor-thirds` is the CLI's own default, so `--pol-sf nnpdfpol` at defaults was bit-identical to `toy` on four channels while `meta` wrote the backend name (measured 2026-09-05, 600 events seed 7, sha256 over all 47 columns and both σ vectors). Neither is refused; both are labelled, and the run says so instead of recording a backend (`pol_sf_is_read(config, plan)`, row 44 and the knob-provenance row above). Values: `toy` (default, the kernel's own `ToyG1` on its own `UnpolSF` and `r_func`, **bit for bit**), `nnpdfpol` (`LhapdfG1("NNPDFpol11_100", 0)` — already `LhapdfG1`'s own default, and the **only polarised set installed** in this tree's LHAPDF store, so there is no second row to offer) or `Custom`. g₂ needs no selector of its own: `PolSF::g2p/g2n` is Wandzura–Wilczek on **this backend's** g₁, so one field moves both consistently. An EXPLICIT `g1_model` keeps whatever it was built with, so this flag moves g₁ and leaves F₁ — and the unpolarised rate — bit for bit. The shared shift is **y-DEPENDENT** where the numerator-only one is y-independent to 2 ulp: at x = 0.05, Q² = 2.5 it runs **−5.4705 % at y = 0.1 → −3.8357 % at y = 0.5 → +2.3678 % at y = 0.9**, straddling zero, so a `--r-source r1998` number must carry its y. (5) divides by F₁ᵈ built from F₂ᵈ = (F₂ᵖ+F₂ⁿ)/2, confirmed by inverting their Table II in all six bins), CDKS say "per nucleon" in words under their Eq. (16) and carry an explicit 1/A in Eq. (10), while Miller's Eqs. (1)/(5)/(6)/(20) are per deuteron with every ½ spoken for. So `CdksB1` **doubled** and `MillerB1` did not; Miller's half is *likely, not certain*, because his own Table I and Fig. 5 compare his curve to HERMES at face value. **Li6B1** = transfer × per_nucleon × b₁(d), defaults 0.921947 × (2/6); no published ⁶Li b₁ exists, so the embedded-deuteron scaling is an inference. Band on the transfer: 0.921947 (rank-2, default) vs the retired vector-rank 0.87. The auto-built Inclusive kernel uses `Li6B1(MillerB1())` for **every** spin-1 ion the channel accepts, the bare deuteron included — a nucleus with no α core receives the ⁶Li transfer. `close_kumano_integral` reports (does not enforce) ∫b₁ dx on the raw columns over the digitized range only — per deuteron for Miller, per nucleon for CDKS, so the two returns are not comparable to each other | [Miller14] Fig. 5 and Eqs. Measured x·b₁ per nucleon at Q² = 2.5 GeV² (raw digitized CDKS b₁ᵈ, `ToyF2` F₁ with `r_sigma_lt`, κ = 1, N_αd = 0.819481): **+3.65e−06 / −2.80e−06 / −2.12e−05 / −4.17e−05 / +5.17e−05** at x = 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.30 / 0.50. **The orbital terms (2d)+(2α) carry the OPPOSITE sign to term (1) across the whole window and are 7–133 % of it**, nearly cancelling it at x ≈ 0.1 and dominating below — the ⁶Li quadrupole puzzle showing up in b₁, and the quantitative reason the 100 % band is mandatory. (2α)/(2d) = 0.4936–0.5035 against the analytic 2(M_d/M_α)² = 0.5064; SD dominates DD by 11.5×, as it must (DD is O(W²), SD is O(UW)). The dropped P₂/P₄ angular remainders are measured, not assumed: below **6e−5** of term (1) everywhere. ∫b₁ dx over [0.01, 1.2] = **+1.459e−04** — reported, never enforced. | The A = 2 validation gate of that convolution — **PASSED 2026-09-03**, and what it does and does not license | The same kernel fed the AV18 deuteron u(k), w(k) and a nucleon F₁ must reproduce CDKS Fig. 4; `DeuteronConvolutionB1` is that gate and `b1_landmarks` computes the comparison (never types it). Layer 0 (analytic, G0a–G0g), layer 1 (unpolarized convolution, G1a–G1f) and the α–d sign gate **PASS**. **G3a passes** — with MSTW2008 LO at CDKS Eq. (21) the zeros are 0.0279 (falling) and 0.4952 (rising) against the digitized 0.0656 / 0.4572 and the peak sits at 0.7716 against 0.7657, i.e. misses of 0.038 / 0.038 / 0.0059 against tolerances 0.08 / 0.10 / 0.10; the counting window is **(0, 1.0]** and the scan grid `linspace(0.001, 1.59, 300)`, both stated in the design since 2026-09-03 (the previous [0.02, 1.0] window was narrower than the ±0.08 tolerance it bracketed, which made that tolerance dead code). **G3a's pass is QUALIFIED by that window's upper edge**, which no tolerance derives: over the digitized reference's own domain [0.010, 1.590] every computed configuration has a **third** sign change (1.2204 ToyF2 / 1.2177 MSTW / 1.1977 CT18NLO, pinned by `tests/test_b1_nuclear.cpp` T17) while the reference stays positive from 0.4572 to its last point — so "exactly two" is never to be quoted without the window (`docs/OPEN_ITEMS_SOLUTIONS.md` §10, "G3a's stated limitation"). **G3b passes**: max \|x·b₁\| over [0.10, 0.80] = **9.15096e−4** against the digitized 1.08521e−3, ratio **0.843243**, a factor 1.19 low, inside [0.5, 2] with the lower edge cleared by a factor 1.69. G3c (Close–Kumano, reported not enforced) is +2.24896e−4 against +4.59200e−4. **Read the conditions with the verdict.** (i) **The lift is about a CONFIGURATION, not about a build.** It is read at the END of design §5.4's checklist: item 4 is the nucleon PDF and MSTW2008 LO is CDKS's own, reached through `MstwSF`. The **shipped default** unpolarised backend is `ToyF2`, whose own G3b is **0.440** — *outside* the [0.5, 2] window. Since 2026-09-04 the passing configuration is emittable from the run surface (`PipelineConfig::b1_unpol` / `--b1-unpol mstw`, which threads `MstwSF` into `Li6ConvolutionOptions::unpol` through `default_inclusive_kernel`), so the rule is **quote numbers made with `--b1-unpol mstw`; the default `toy` backend's numbers are not covered by the lift**, and they cannot be rescaled into covered ones — mstw/toy on `Li6ConvolutionB1::b1(x, 2.5)` is 1.848 / 1.276 / 0.817 at x = 0.10 / 0.30 / 0.50, a shape change and not a normalisation. A build without the PYTHIA tier cannot reproduce the verdict row and `validate()` refuses the selector there rather than downgrading it, so it also cannot emit a number that claims the verdict. (ii) At Eq. (17)'s κ = 1 the same row is **0.520** — inside by 4 % of its own value, so "the gate passes" without "at Eq. (21)" oversells it. (iii) With CD-Bonn as well (`Options::wave = kCdBonn`, CDKS's own wave function) the ratio is **1.000338**, the zeros 0.0641 / 0.4570 and the dip −1.769065e−4 against −1.768140e−4 — that is agreement below the error of digitizing a published figure, is specific to CD-Bonn **and** MSTW together, and is **not** the default. (iv) The gate is **A = 2**: it validates the kernel on the deuteron and says nothing about the α–d step, so the mandatory ±100 % band on every ⁶Li number stays. Design §5.4's *Escalation* clause — "if **after the checklist** the peak ratio is still outside a factor of 2" — is therefore not triggered and the ⁶Li publication ban it imposed is lifted. Two conventions differ deliberately between the two objects: `DeuteronConvolutionB1::Options::finite_q_delta` defaults **true** (this object exists to reproduce CDKS's own figure and Eq. (21) is their exact definition — worth a factor 1.62 there), while `Li6ConvolutionOptions::finite_q_delta` defaults **false**, where the same switch moves x·b₁ by only −1 % to +7 % and buys one cached f(y) table against a 0.24 s rebuild per x | [CDKS17] Fig. 4 and Eqs. (17)/(18)/(21) (the digitized column is `tables::kB1CdksQ2p5`); the gate as rerun, clause by clause, in `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-03/phase_A_numbers.md` (§§0-5 the MSTW rerun, §8 CD-Bonn) and `phase_A_cdbonn.md`; the superseded failing verdict in `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-02/phase_D_gate.md`; `docs/USAGE.md` §2a | `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:664` `DeuteronConvolutionB1`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:702` `finite_q_delta`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:546` `finite_q_delta`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:730` `B1Landmarks`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:737` `b1_landmarks`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:739` `b1_landmarks_of_table`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:758` `alpha_d_quadrupole_fm2`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:647` `DeuteronWaveSource`; `include/lipolgen/b1_nuclear.hpp:671` `wave`; `include/lipolgen/mstw_sf.hpp:66` `MstwSF`; `include/lipolgen/cluster.hpp:243` `CdBonnWave`; `include/lipolgen/cluster.hpp:278` `cdbonn_wave`; `include/lipolgen/cluster.hpp:294` `cdbonn_fdeut_table`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:459` `B1UnpolSource`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:917` `b1_unpol` |

`cdks` puts the same ⁶Li rank-2 transfer on the CDKS camp; `li6-convolution` is the four-term backend above. **Both opt-in backends carry a MANDATORY 100 % band**: `--b1-band-scale` (any non-negative float; the band is the three rows 0 / 1 / 2) multiplies the WHOLE b₁ (all four terms of the convolution; the scale-2 row is bit-for-bit 2× the scale-1 row) and a single row is **not a result** — Q(⁶Li) = −0.0818(17) fm² (`LI6_QUADRUPOLE_FM2`, TUNL A = 6, 1998CE04; Pyykkö's compilation gives −0.0806(6)) against Q_d = +0.2859(3) fm² is exactly the near-cancellation the band expresses. `--b1-alpha-d-dwave-weight` is the SHAPE variant of the same worry, scaling (2d) and (2α) together and linear by construction (**that last reading is TOY-ONLY and was WITHDRAWN on 2026-09-06**, when the ⁶Li tables were regenerated as a band: at x = 0.10 the orbital term is 70 % of the total *on the shipped `ToyF2`*, so w = 2 nearly zeroes b₁ there — but on `--b1-unpol ct18nlo` and on `mstw`, w = 2 moves x·b₁(0.10) **away** from zero by +24 % and +9 %, and the knob stays exactly linear on all three); report it separately from the band. **The tables the band rows come from were regenerated on 2026-09-06 as a BAND over `--b1-unpol {toy, ct18nlo, mstw}` × `--unpol-sf {toy, ct18nlo, mstw}`** and may be quoted only as that envelope inside the mandatory ±100 %: x·b₁ at Q² = 2.5 spans [+1.574759e−6, +3.650716e−6] / [−6.212120e−6, −2.796612e−6] / [−2.803165e−5, −2.120522e−5] / [−5.324901e−5, −4.173248e−5] / [+4.226952e−5, +5.411263e−5] at x = 0.05/0.10/0.20/0.30/0.50, a backend spread ×2.318 / ×2.221 / ×1.322 / ×1.276 / ×1.280 — **wider than the ±100 % band itself at x ≤ 0.1**, so `{0, 1, 2}` on one backend does not bracket the others there. `--unpol-sf` is exactly flat on x·b₁ (`Li6ConvolutionB1::b1` ignores its `f1`), so the band is three wide, not seven, and two of the nine cells are refused by name. `PipelineConfig::validate()` **refuses every combination that would record a variation which did not run**: a band scale or an α–d weight ≠ 1.0 on `miller` (whose numbers are published and deliberately unbanded), an α–d weight on `cdks` (which never reads it), a non-Miller model together with a caller-supplied `kernel` (the kernel would silently win), and either opt-in model on anything but the **inclusive** channel and the **⁶Li** isotope — on a tagged channel the α–d density is already in the event weight, on the coherent channel the tensor signal lives in the recoil azimuth, and the deuteron is refused by name with a pointer to `DeuteronConvolutionB1`. Two further systematics are quoted but have no CLI switch: `norm_target` bands the α–d spectroscopic factor by ±5 % (b₁ is exactly linear in it; three tabulations span 0.819481, 0.856 and 0.863 and **the spread is not explained**), and `use_spectroscopic_factor = false` — the "all of ⁶Li is α + d" reading — multiplies everything by 1/N_αd = **1.2203**. The moment-constrained alternative normalizes the shape to peak 1 and solves A from the bag-model moment ∫x Δ dx = C_BAG·α_s(Q²) with C_BAG = −0.012, either through a 220+120-point log/linear trapezoid grid (Interpretation A, keeps F₁) or analytically through the Beta function (Interpretation B, drops F₁). α_s defaults to the LO analytic form 12π/[(33−2n_f)ln(Q²/Λ²)] with Λ = 0.22, n_f = 4, explicitly a **fallback**: the production convention is the CT18NLO α_s table, which makes the moment-constrained Δ about 14 % smaller in magnitude. `dilution` (default 1) is the counting fraction (1/3 for ⁶Li, "two polarized nucleons of six"), explicitly **not** a polarization and not the same as the rank-2 transfer applied to b₁. ## 4. Sampling, RNG, run-plan bookkeeping, estimators and the T0 event record

Randomness is **counter-based**, never a global stream: `Rng(seed, run, bunch,
event)` is an xoshiro256\*\* generator seeded by folding all four `uint64`
arguments through splitmix64, so every event of a (seed, run, bunch) triple gets
its own generator and a batch is bit-identical at any thread count and
independent of generation order. Three luminosity factors multiply and must never
be confused: the **programme share** (`Scenario::run_share`), the **optics
fraction** (what a de-squeezed β\*_x costs, ≈1/7–1/13 for lithium tagging) and
the **spin-state share** (`SpinCategory::lumi_fraction`, summing to one within a
plan). A share moves COUNTS, never CROSS SECTIONS.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rate → event counts | Per spin category, μ = L·σ_tot(cat) with σ_tot = Σ_m p_m σ_state(cat,m); the count is Poisson (independent Poissons per spin state are one Poisson total plus a multinomial over m, which is what the per-event m draw does). `rng_poisson` uses Knuth's product method below mean 30 and Hörmann's PTRS transformed rejection at/above it (**the PTRS branch is the algorithm NumPy uses; the 30 crossover is this repo's own choice — NumPy switches at 10 — and has no cited source**). The count draw is pinned to the reserved counter `kCountStreamEvent` (all ones), so appending events never perturbs it; `poisson = false` rounds μ instead. In fixed-`--events` mode the total is split as floor(N·lumi_fraction·σ/Σ) with the leftover handed out **one per category in plan order from k = 0** (the code comment calls this "largest-remainder", but remainders are never ranked). On the CLI/Pipeline path the luminosity mode multiplies in the optics factor first: μ_k = L·f_optics·lumi_fraction_k·σ_k with f_optics = `Optics::lumi_fraction` when `apply_optics_lumi_fraction` (default true; ignored in `--events` mode) — 1 for the Yellow-Report envelopes, 0.147 at the ⁶Li 5×41 tagging point | plans/05 §5.2 item 4 ("Sampling"), quoted at `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:12` as "5.2.4"; the plan file lives outside this repository | `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:133` `rng_poisson`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:82` `rng_poisson`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:296` `sample_lumi`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:485` `sample_lumi`; `include/lipolgen/bookkeeping.hpp:244` `kCountStreamEvent`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:249` `sigma_tot_pb`; `include/lipolgen/generator.hpp:130` `run`; `src/core/generator.cpp:241` `run_n`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:1811` `optics_lumi_factor`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:76` `--events`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:78` `--lumi` |
| (x, Q²) inverse-CDF grid and the acceptance window | A fixed log–log grid (default 100×72 cells, x ∈ [1e-4,1], Q² ∈ [1,2000] GeV²) with NumPy-`logspace` edges and geometric-mean centres. Every cell centre is filtered by `in_acceptance` (Q² ≥ Q²_min, x ≤ x_max, y = Q²/(sx) ∈ [y_min,y_max], W² = M_N² + Q²(1−x)/x ≥ W²_min, and the scattered-electron pseudorapidity and energy inside the window); an accepted cell keeps σ_cell = dσ/dxdQ²·Δx·ΔQ², share-invariant by construction (multiplied by, then divided back out by, the effective luminosity). Per spin state a cumulative table cdf[i] = Σ σ_cell(1+w_avg) is built and the cell drawn by inverse CDF; inside the cell (x,Q²) is placed LOG-UNIFORM and redrawn up to 21 times until it lands back inside the window, falling back to the cell centre on 21 failures. **Analysis `Scenario` defaults**: 10 fb⁻¹/u, run_share 1, placeholder polarizations 0.70/0.70/0.60, Q² ≥ 1, y ∈ [0.01,0.95], W² ≥ 10, x ≤ 1, \|η\| ≤ 3.5, E′ ≥ 0.5 GeV. **`PipelineConfig::scenario` defaults to `generator_scenario(Scenario())`**: Q² ≥ 0.7, y ∈ [0.004,0.985], W² ≥ 8, η ∈ [−3.8,3.8], E′ ≥ 0.3 GeV — looser than any analysis window in y, W², η and E′ so events can migrate in, **but not in Q²**: the effective Q² floor of a default run is the GRID's own `q2_min` = 1.0 GeV² (`include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:156` `q2_min`), where the log edges start (`src/core/sampler.cpp:42` `q2_edges`), and `PipelineConfig::grid = SamplerGrid()` is handed verbatim to the inclusive sampler (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:1646` `InclusiveSampler`) and copied onto the struck-cluster one (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:1531` `grid`). The scenario's 0.7 GeV² is therefore INERT — every generated Q² is ≥ 1.0 GeV² — until `PipelineConfig::grid.q2_min` is lowered with it. Fidelity note: amplitudes are evaluated at cell CENTRES, adequate for binned Phase-1 estimators, not for unbinned in-cell shapes | plans/05 §5.2 item 4; `docs/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` §5 for the generator window | `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:81` `log_grid`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:36` `log_grid`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:152` `SamplerGrid`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:156` `q2_min`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:92` `Scenario`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:59` `validate`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:124` `generator_scenario`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:188` `in_acceptance`; `include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:74` `scattered_electron`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:18` `scattered_electron`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:389` `draw_event`; `src/core/sampler.cpp:138` `InclusiveSampler` |
| Azimuthal accept–reject with a hard positivity guard | φ′ is drawn by accept–reject on 1 + a1n cos φ′ + a2n cos 2φ′ (amplitudes normalized by 1+w_avg at the cell centre) against the exact envelope 1 + \|a1n\| + \|a2n\|; the lab azimuth is φ = φ′ + φ_S mod 2π, φ being the SCATTERED-ELECTRON azimuth about the ion (+z) axis and φ′ exactly the covariant φ_S of the alignment axis for a massless target. Before any event is drawn, `build_state` computes the exact minimum of the normalized density over φ for every accepted cell and **THROWS** if it is negative anywhere — sampling max(density,0) would both dilute the modulation and skew the (x,Q²) mixture, since the cell weights assume the per-cell φ integral is 2π(1+w_avg). With it off the vector a₁ amplitude is not computed — it needs g₂ — so a1n ≡ 0, the envelope is 1 + \|a2n\| and the φ′ density is 1 + a2n cos 2φ′. The inclusive `Pipeline` never sets it, so **the CLI inclusive channel has no cos φ′ modulation**; it is reachable from Python, and on the tagged channels through `StruckClusterOptions::with_perp` (also false by default). ## 5. Tagged (incoherent, spectator-tagged) channels

Each beam nucleus is modelled as a bound two-cluster system. The ion ground state
is expanded as

    |J M> = Σ_L a_L Σ_{m_L,m_S} <L m_L S_c m_S|J M> ψ̂_L(k) Y_L^{m_L}(k̂) |S_c m_S> ,

giving the joint spin ⊗ orbital amplitude
A_{m_S}(M;k,k̂) = Σ_L a_L ψ̂_L(k) C_L(M,m_S) Θ_L^{M−m_S}(cos θ_k), with the
azimuth-stripped harmonic Θ (Condon–Shortley signs) because only cos θ_k enters.
The spectator density is n_M = Σ_{m_S}|A_{m_S}|² and the struck-cluster spin
population p(m_S|M,k,k̂) = |A_{m_S}|²/n_M — a **diagonal truncation**: coherences
are dropped, and this is documented, not computed. Masses are physical
AME2020-derived nuclear masses (never A·M_u), so the k = 0 rigidity
R = (m_spec/Z_spec)/(m_beam/Z_beam) is 0.99813 for ⁶Li → α (not 1) and 0.85571
for ⁷Li → α (not 6/7), and κ = √(2μS) with μ the reduced mass of the two free
clusters and S the separation energy.

The tagged `vector_dilution` of the Hulthén ⁶Li model is the same quantity computed from the same S+D wave — which is why P_D lives in `beams.hpp` and not here, and since 2026-09-04 that identity is **measured**: **0.8699431789** (tagged grid quadrature) against 0.869950 (closed form), **7.84e−6** relative — 6.82e−6 absolute. *(Re-measured 2026-09-06 on the fixed S–D interference sign, which moves the quadrature residual by +4.3e−6; the pre-fix pair read 0.869939 and **1.22e−5**.)* **Under `--cluster-wave vmc` the tagged P_D becomes 0.0193 while the inclusive effective polarization still uses 0.0867: in that mode the two genuinely differ — by +11.61 % in the vector sector and +6.58 % in the rank-2 one** (`LI6_B1_RANK2_TRANSFER` = 0.921947 against the VMC channel's `tensor_dilution` = 0.982576). **It is NOT closed by substitution, and that is the finding** (open item C5.5, `phase_C_numbers.md` §C5.5): feeding `VMC_P_D_LI6` into the product gives `LI6_CLUSTER_POLARIZATION_VMC` = 0.905427, **6.8 % above** the ab-initio six-body VMC `LI6_POLARIZATION_VMC_SIX_BODY` = 0.848, where the shipped 0.811228 is 4.3 % **below** it; with the AV18 deuteron's own P_D = 0.057600 as well it is 0.887076, still 4.6 % above. The virtuality M_X² − m_free² is carried explicitly rather than hidden. Light-front variables are quoted in the ion rest frame: α_s = A_beam(E_k − k_z)/m_beam, α_x = A_beam − α_s, p_{T,s} = \|k_T\|. **Convention caveat**: with k_z the rest-frame component along the beam (+z), this α_s is the light-front MINUS fraction A·p_s⁻/P_ion⁻, which has the opposite k_z sign to the Strikman–Weiss/Cosyn–Weiss α_p = A(E_k + k_z)/m; the marginal α_s distribution is unaffected (n_M is even in cos θ_k) but α_s is anti-correlated with the x_L and pz_lab on the same record. α_s(k=0) = A_beam·m_spec/m_beam = 3.9925 (⁶Li α) / 3.9933 (⁷Li α), i.e. ≈A_spec, not exactly A_spec | [CW20], [CW26] (polarized tagged DIS in the impulse approximation); [BeAGLE22] §II and `docs/surveys/beagle_survey.md:120-127`. ## 6. Tier T1: resolving the struck cluster into a struck nucleon plus partners

For any tagged channel at `Tier::T1` the off-shell struck cluster P_X (already
off shell because the tagged spectator was put on shell one level up) is split
into ON-SHELL partner spectators plus a struck nucleon that absorbs whatever
four-momentum is left:

    p_N,struck = P_X − Σ p_partner ,   so   p_N,struck² ≠ M_N² ,

built from the four-vectors actually emitted, so that
k + P_ion = k′ + p_spec + Σp_partner + hadrons closes exactly to rounding
whatever internal model generated the partners. This is BeAGLE's "never
recoil-correct the light spectator" rule applied a second time. **T1 is the
default on tagged channels; there is no CLI switch for the tier** — `--plan`
selects the *spin* run plan and has nothing to do with tiers — and `Tier::T0`
(the cluster left as one off-shell pseudo-particle) is kept only for
bit-compatibility with numbers published before T1 existed.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| The impulse-approximation closure rule | As above; the virtuality p_N,struck² − M_N² is stored explicitly. No FSI between fragments, no nuclear transparency and no formation-time effect is applied anywhere in this closure — only the four-momentum balance is exact, never the shape | [BeAGLE22]; [CW20]/[CW26] for the spectator-on-shell/struck-off-shell impulse approximation (`include/lipolgen/tagged.hpp:373-384`, `docs/surveys/beagle_survey.md:120-127`) | `include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:269` `resolve`; `src/core/breakup.cpp:190` `resolve`; `include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:198` `virtuality`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:301` `Tier` |

Steps: draw m_sc from P(m_sc\|m_S); draw (k, cos θ_k, φ_k); draw which nucleon is struck by the Z·F₂ᵖ : N·F₂ⁿ rule at Z = N = 1; put the PARTNER on shell at +k in the P_X rest frame; draw the pair's two spin projections TOGETHER from p(m₁,m₂\|m_sc) = \|⟨½m₁ ½m₂\|1 m_sc⟩\|² so the m_sc = 0 anticorrelation of the deuteron triplet survives sampling — the effective nucleon polarization ⟨2m₁⟩ = (1 − 1.5 P_D)·m_S then *emerges* from the draw rather than being imposed. **β = 0.30 GeV** is a momentum-space scale (band 0.20–0.40 GeV, `--cluster-beta`); **P_D (0.045) has a CLI lever, `--p-d` (`python/lipolgen/cli.py:101`) — this row said it had none until 2026-09-05 — while κ_nn (10.44 MeV) genuinely has none**; neither is fixed in the library: they are the `BreakupOptions` fields `p_d` and `kappa_nn` (`include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:211` `p_d`; `include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:231` `kappa_nn`), `p_d` being what this deuteron model is actually built at (`src/core/breakup.cpp:141` `p_d`) on the struct's own `nk` × `nc` = 280 × 96 grid (`include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:233` `nk`) and `kappa_nn` the nn virtual-state pole of the ⁷Li triton path below; the `Pipeline` constructor overwrites only `beta`, a null `f2` and a null `triton_sf` (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:1616` `beta`), so both stay free through `PipelineConfig::breakup` in C++ and Python. κ_d = 45.7 MeV is not a field at all — it is √(2μS) from the AME2020 masses. ## 7. Spectator final-state interaction: a weight, never a shift

The spectator four-vector is the measurement and is never moved. Rescattering
enters only as a multiplicative weight: `weight(kin)` is the FSI/IA density ratio
(≥ 0, 1 = no distortion), `weight_normalised(kin)` divides it by its rate-weighted
mean so the SHAPE distortion is applied without changing the total rate, and
`sigma_eff_mb(W)` records the σ_XN used. `PipelineFsi::Off` — the default — is
today's plane-wave impulse approximation bit for bit. **What the pipeline
actually applies is the raw `weight()`, not `weight_normalised()`**, and
`Pipeline::sigma_pb()` is not rescaled: an FSI-on run keeps the PWIA cross
section while its event weights average to the survival probability (0.520 at
40 mb on the ⁶Li S+D tag), so the integrated tagged rate is reduced by that
factor. FSI is refused outside the three tagged channels.

At EIC energies rescattering is eikonal — it transfers only transverse momentum,
so k_z (equivalently α_s) is a spectator of the FSI. The distortion is a 2-D
convolution at fixed k_z,

    Ψ_FSI(k_z,k_T) = ψ(k_z,k_T) − ∫ d²k′_T/(2π)² G̃(k_T−k′_T) ψ(k_z,k′_T) ,

the momentum-space form of Ψ_FSI(b) = ψ(b)[1−Γ(b)] with G̃(0) = σ_Xa(1−iε)/2. It
is implemented on an (L,m) partial-wave basis, and the weight
w = Σ_{L,m}(P_L/(2L+1))\|g^FSI_{Lm}\|² / Σ_{L,m}(P_L/(2L+1))\|g_{Lm}\|² is summed
over m and averaged over the ion projection M, so the S–D interference cancels
and **the weight is spin-independent by construction** — an unpolarized-shape
systematic, never a correction to A_zz.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| The interface contract | `weight` / `weight_normalised` / `sigma_eff_mb`; no RNG is consumed building or applying it, and an FSI-on run is bit-identical to FSI-off in **every four-vector** — only the weight column moves (proved event by event in `tests/test_fsi.cpp`). The weight reaches HepMC3 `weights()[0]` and the npz `weight` column with no further wiring. The whole model is configured by one plain struct, `GlauberFsiOptions` (`include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:275` `GlauberFsiOptions`), of which a Pipeline/CLI run sets exactly two fields — `variant` (`include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:277` `variant`) from `--fsi` and `sigma_xn_mb` (`include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:282` `sigma_xn_mb`) from `--fsi-sigma-mb` — every other field keeping its default | `docs/USAGE.md` §3 "FSI of the DIS debris with the spectator" | `include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:258` `FsiWeight`; `include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:263` `weight`; `include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:267` `weight_normalised`; `include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:270` `sigma_eff_mb`; `include/lipolgen/fsi.hpp:415` `PipelineFsi`; `src/core/fsi.cpp:72` `pipeline_fsi_name`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:1374` `fsi_weight`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:182` `--fsi` |

## 8. Tensor-sector radiative corrections: a weight family, never a shift

`--rc tensor-band` (**off by default**) adds three per-event weights to
`Event::rc_weights` and touches nothing else: no four-vector moves, `Event::weight`
is untouched, `Pipeline::sigma_pb()` is not rescaled, and an `--rc off` run is bit
for bit today's. Two physically distinct objects live in the family. **The band**
`rc_tensor_lo`/`rc_tensor_hi` = 1 ∓ δ(x)·τ prices *the lepton-vertex correction this
generator does not apply*, taken as a 1σ systematic, with τ the rank-2 (tensor)
fraction of the event's own rate. **The tail** `rc_tail` = 1 + σ_tail/σ_Born applies
the ⁶Li elastic and unpolarised quasi-elastic radiative tails as a dilution of A_zz,

    w_tail − 1 = [ σ^el_U + (Q_N/6)·σ^el_T + κ_qe·σ^q_U ] / [ x·s·dσ_unpol·(1 + w_avg) ] ,

with Q_N = P_zz^eff (+1 at m = ±1, −2 at m = 0). The whole family assumes an
**UNPOLARISED beam**: the constructor throws when any run-plan category has
λ_e·P_e ≠ 0, because τ is the rank-2 projection and a polarised beam would price the
vector sector as if it were rank 2.

**The ordering of the two, measured, is the headline.** At x = 0.01, Q² = 5 GeV² on
⁶Li at 10 × 99.5 GeV/u the band's δ_low = 0.19 vs 0.30 edges give half-widths on A_zz
of **2.7996e−04 vs 4.4204e−04**, against a whole-tail shift ΔA_zz of **3.5167e−07** —
a factor **≈ 1260**. At EIC collider kinematics these cells sit at small y (y = Q²/(x s))
where the tail carries Y₊ = [1+(1−y)²]/(1−y) ≈ 2, so the tail is a bookkeeping item
and the unapplied vertex correction is the systematic; at a fixed target the ordering
reverses (the same model gives 1.8 % at y = 0.5 and 33 % at y = 0.9). Every number
below is a measurement of the shipped model, taken from
`docs/open_items/run_2026-09-02/phase_C_numbers.md`.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| The band: δ(x), τ, and its size | `rc_tensor_lo`/`rc_tensor_hi` = 1 ∓ δ(x)·τ. δ(x) is **log-linear between two anchors and clamped outside them**, δ = δ_high + (δ_low − δ_high)·ln(x_high/x)/ln(x_high/x_low), monotone non-increasing, with both anchor values returned EXACTLY: **δ = 0.015 at x ≥ 0.16** (`RC_DELTA_HIGH_X`/`RC_X_HIGH`, the E12-13-011 proposal's own lower x edge) and **δ = 0.30 at x ≤ 0.01** (`RC_DELTA_LOW_X`), passing through δ(0.063) = 0.1108 so that it does not contradict HERMES's measured 15 % residual there. τ = W_tensor/W is the rank-2 fraction of the event's own rate — on the inclusive path from the sampler's state tables, on a tagged channel τ_tag = 1 − n̄(k,c)/n_M(k,c) on the M-dependent spectator density. **BAND IT**: run `--rc-delta-low-x` 0.19 (`RC_DELTA_LOW_X_OPTIMISTIC`, the residual HERMES actually achieved: 2e−3 against a measured \|A_zz\| = 1.06e−2 at ⟨x⟩ = 0.012) *and* 0.30, never one row. **And say which way the shipped anchor errs — PRICED 2026-09-06, registry row 17** (`docs/open_items/run_2026-09-06/phase_A_numbers.md` §A2): the 0.30 is the source panel's value **carried upward in x**, and **0.113 is the value that panel actually reads at x = 0.00966, the x nearest `RC_X_LOW` = 0.01** (0.266 at its own bottom, x = 0.00226), so the default is **×2.65** that reading. Band half-width on A_zz at Q² = 5, P_zz = +1, ⁶Li config 1, this generator's own ⁶Li b₁ (A_zz = −4.528242e−04 / −1.316774e−03 / −1.528923e−03 / −1.280460e−03 at x = 0.010 / 0.063 / 0.100 / 0.160) — at δ_low = **0.30 / 0.266 / 0.113**: **1.358472e−04 / 1.204512e−04 / 5.116913e−05** (x = 0.010), **1.459067e−04 / 1.308566e−04 / 6.313127e−05** (0.063), **9.680016e−05 / 8.798804e−05 / 4.833349e−05** (0.100), **1.920691e−05** on all three (0.160). The anchor is carried in FULL only at x ≤ 0.01 (×0.8867, ×0.3767), **compressed** by the log-linear interpolation between the anchors (×0.8969/×0.4327 at 0.063, ×0.9090/×0.4993 at 0.100) and **identically zero** at x ≥ 0.16, so "the band scales with δ_low" is true at the anchor and false everywhere else. The band still leads the whole radiative tail by **×768 / ×681 / ×289** at x = 0.01, so the RC budget's ordering survives all three; the peak stays at x = 0.063 on all three and sharpens as the anchor drops. Nothing is decided and the default has not moved (`python/tests/test_rc_low_x_anchor.py`). Quote the pair or the span, never the `ho` number alone (`OPEN_ITEMS_SOLUTIONS.md` §9 item 1; `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-02/phase_C_numbers.md` §8.1c item 1; `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-03/phase_B_numbers.md` §B1). Measured against the Weizsäcker–Williams × elastic construction — now the shipped one, so T8(c) gates the code that runs — POLRAD §2.1.3 B's claim is **event-weighted and not per-cell**: in the Q² ≥ 20 GeV², y ≤ 0.9 window the event-weighted mean dilution moves only **+0.62 %** (8.719649e−03 → 8.773752e−03, **5194** of 200 000 events, seed 1234; the 8.48914e−03 → 8.54072e−03 / 5182 this row carried is the SAME build at P_z = 0, the fill both test suites use, and reproduces to every digit there; the numbers here are at the CLI default P_z = 0.7 — the two fills differ, not the build (the earlier "pre-Phase-A, does not reproduce" attribution was false and is withdrawn, 2026-09-15) — and `polrad-full` is **above both** at 8.815031e−03, +1.09 %), while **331 of that window's 1356 accepted cells (24.4 %, 28.2 % of its cross section) disagree by more than 1 %**, worst **×6444** at x = 0.7943, y = 0.0088, Q² = 27.8, and **318 of them sit at y < 0.1**. Two mechanisms, not one: at y = 0.985 it is only **59 %** because `z_s = (1−y)/(1−x_A y) → 0` puts the elastic vertex at Q′² → 0 where the form factor is 1, and at y → 0 it is the **uncancelled soft radiator** — `1 − z_s = y(1−x_A)/(1−x_A y)`, so `D(z_s) ∝ 1/y` reaches 11.5 at x = 0.72 — against a t-peak whose own vertex is stuck above `t_min ∝ x²` and which the Q² ≥ 20 cut pushes to high x. Elsewhere it is false outright: only **23 %** at the HERMES deuteron point (low by a factor **4.36** on the leading-log estimate and by **2.36** on the exact tail, i.e. the upper edge overshoots by 1.85×), and at the low-Q² corner of the generator window (x = 0.01, y = 0.1, Q² ≈ 4 GeV²) the quasi-elastic **s+p** is **3.35×** the t-peak unsuppressed and **7.09×** at the shipped Pauli k_F (the s-peak **alone** is 2.33× / 4.94× — the pre-2026-09-03 text said "s-peak … 3.3×" and conflated the two). | The ⁶Li spin-1 elastic form factors F_c, F_m, F_q | `Spin1ElasticFF` is the abstract three-form-factor interface (charge, magnetic, quadrupole) plus a `provenance()` string the run banner prints. The shipped implementation `HoSpin1FF` is a **PHENOMENOLOGICAL FIT, not a shell model**: a harmonic-oscillator point charge F_point(a = 1.9069 fm, α = 0.13822), a magnetic shape F_mag(q_z = 1.30 fm⁻¹, b = 1.85 fm), the quadrupole term normalised to the **measured** moments μ = 0.822047 μ_N and Q = −0.0818 fm² (**not** VMC — WS98's Q(⁶Li) = −0.23(9) fm² is 3× the measured one), all folded with the isoscalar G_E^p + G_E^n of `nucleon_ff` (dipole plus Galster, N = Z), at Z = 3 and the AME nuclear mass M_A = 5.60152 GeV. **The parameters are UNFITTED STARTING VALUES and are shipped as such**: the Suelzle–Yearian–Crannell (PR 162 (1967) 992) and Li–Sick–Whitney–Yearian (NPA 162 (1971) 583) elastic data are not in this repository in any machine-readable form, so there was nothing to fit against; they do reproduce their two design targets exactly, ⟨r²⟩_point = `LI6_R2_POINT_FM2` = 6.0788 fm² and a first C0 zero at 3.0998 fm⁻¹ (q₀ = (1/a)√(2(2+3α)/α) = 3.09977; `phase_C_numbers.md` rounds it to 3.0999). **Three** band knobs. `fq_scale` (±100 % on F_q) and `tail_tensor_scale` (on F_m, i.e. the η·F_m² tensor sector, which `fq_scale` does **not** span) are multipliers; the third, added 2026-09-04, is a **SHAPE**: `C0Shape` = `Ho` (the default, above) or `VmcFt`, the j₀ transform of the committed ANL VMC point-proton density `data/vmc/density/li6.density`, r-rescaled by 1.008959 so that it carries the **same** measured ⟨r²⟩_point and therefore the same F_c(0) = 3 and F_q(0) = −65.914 (T11 gates all three on both edges). It moves F_c and F_q **together** — they share one monopole — so it cannot be rescaled out of one run: **run both edges**. It is not a small band: F_point differs by ×2.51 at q = 2 fm⁻¹ and ×6.15 at 2.5 (×6.3, ×37.8 in F²), no two-parameter harmonic oscillator can be refitted to reach that while holding ⟨r²⟩, and the `VmcFt` edge has **no C0 zero at any q** where the `Ho` edge's F_c and F_q both change sign at q₀ = 3.0999 fm⁻¹. What that costs: (1/6)σ^el_T/σ^el_U at Q² = 5 GeV² is −5.0943e−04 / **−5.4705e−04** at x = 0.01, +1.5595e−04 / **−4.0702e−05** at x = 0.10 and +1.3287e−02 / **+1.5556e−02** at x = 0.30 (ho / vmc-ft), i.e. **the sign at x = 0.10 is a band edge and not a result** — `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-03/phase_B_numbers.md` §B1. σ^el_U rises by ×1.011 / ×1.022 / ×1.104 / ×3.03 at x = 0.01 / 0.03 / 0.10 / 0.30 and the quasi-elastic tail is untouched. The high-q continuation (above q = 3 fm⁻¹, a pure exponential at the transform's own [2, 3] fm⁻¹ log-slope λ = 3.4048 fm) is its **own** model choice, argued in the header and shown there to move nothing: q_cut = 2.5 fm⁻¹ or a Gaussian law changes every row by < 0.1 %. The edge costs 1.33 s of `RcModel` construction against 0.146 s on `Ho`, and `meta["rc_c0_shape"]` records which one ran. **σ^el_T is QUADRATIC in F_q**, so the band must be RUN (0, 1, 2) and never rescaled from one run — and it **changes the sign of ΔA_zz** (+7.54154e−07 at `fq_scale` = 0 → −4.9925e−08 at 2, spread 8.041e−07), while `tail_tensor_scale` 0.5 → 2 spreads only 3.61e−08. `TabulatedSpin1FF` (a digitised `data/ff/` (q, F_C0, F_C2, F_M1) table, log-linear in q, refusing to extrapolate) exists as the alternative `Spin1ElasticFF`, but **no table is shipped and nothing selects it automatically**: it reaches the model only as a caller-supplied `RcOptions::ff` built by `TabulatedSpin1FF::from_data_dir` (`tests/test_rc.cpp` pins that `ff/li6_elastic.csv` is absent). A caller-supplied `ff` makes `fq_scale` ignored, with a printed line | the parameters and their unfitted status, `include/lipolgen/rc.hpp:383-465` and `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-02/phase_C_numbers.md` §8.3 (open items Q1 and Q10). | Per-channel legality: where the band applies, where the tail does, and why | `RcModel` decides at construction, `applies()` / `tail_applies()` / `exclusion_reason()` report, and the run prints the reason — `PipelineConfig::validate()` deliberately does **not** refuse any channel, so a channel scan need not special-case `--rc`. **Inclusive**: both apply (the tail only when `with_tail`; `with_tail = false` is a band-only diagnostic run that must be labelled so). **The three tagged channels**: the band applies as an **UNCITED extrapolation** — no RC calculation exists for a tagged tensor asymmetry — while **`rc_tail` ≡ 1 exactly**, because the elastic recoil sits at x_L = 1 inside the 10σ beam-exclusion envelope while the tag looks at x_L ≈ A_spec/A_beam, so the tag itself vetoes it. **That is HALF a kinematic fact.** The ELASTIC half is one; the QUASI-ELASTIC half is an **OMISSION**, and the veto argument does not hold for it: quasi-elastic knockout removes one nucleon, the A−1 remnant is **unbound** (⁵Li and ⁵He are resonances above the α + N threshold, with no particle-stable state at all), and its α emerges at x_L ≈ (4/5)(5/6) = **2/3 — the tag window itself**. Sharper: in the α + d picture, when the struck nucleon is one of the embedded deuteron's two the α is a **TRUE SPECTATOR** carrying the same n_M(k, c) the tagged Born is built on (on the deuteron control the tail simply **is** elastic e–n scattering with a spectator proton, the classic spectator-tagging background). So the tag neither vetoes it nor suppresses it in the **ratio** — the same spectator density and Roman-Pot acceptance multiply the tail and the tagged Born, and both select the **2 of 6** nucleons inside the deuteron, while a nucleon knocked out of the **α** destroys the α and *is* vetoed. The omitted dilution is therefore of the **same order as the inclusive quasi-elastic one** (99.9 % of the inclusive tail at x = 0.30), not a negligible one; the design's "probably small" is withdrawn. It stays unimplemented because it needs a **tagged** Born denominator and the tag acceptance folded into Eq. (44) — plus the **cluster-elastic** e + A → e' + γ + d + α that Eq. (44)'s free-nucleon sum does not contain at all — and `exclusion_reason()` now says all of it, in the banner and in `meta`, so `rc_tail ≡ 1` cannot be read as a veto on the whole tail (`run_2026-09-03/phase_B_numbers.md` §B4). A tagged run must be built through the `TaggedModel` constructor or it **throws**: `StruckClusterOptions::inclusive_b1` is off by default, so the struck cluster's kernel carries no b₁ and the inclusive recipe would silently return τ = 0 and price nothing. **Coherent ⁶Li**: neither applies and both weights are exactly 1.0 — its tensor dependence is entirely AZIMUTHAL (the cos 2φ coefficient of `CoherentSampler`), nobody has computed RC for a φ-dependent tensor observable at any axis, and its own M_X floor already excludes the elastic point. ## 9. Coherent channel: e + ⁶Li → e′ + X + ⁶Li(g.s.)

The nucleus stays in its 1⁺ ground state and recoils with \|t\| ≈ p_T². The
header is explicit that **everything in this channel is a SCENARIO with bands, to
be replaced by a real diffractive model** — no published coherent-diffraction
calculation exists for any polarized A > 2 nucleus. The rate is carried by a
Lorentzian coherent fraction f_coh(x) = f₀/(1 + (x/x_coh)²) with f₀ = 0.04
(band 0.02–0.08) and x_coh = 0.01; the form factor is Gaussian,
\|F(t)\|² = exp(−B\|t\|) with B = R_rms²/3, default B = 50 GeV⁻² (band {40,60}).
Because the intact ⁶Li has A/Z = 2, exactly the beam rigidity, the only
far-forward handle is the Roman-Pot near-beam p_T tail, and the tag acceptance is
analytic: acc = exp(−B p_{T,cut}²), with ⟨\|t\|⟩ of the tagged sample
= p_{T,cut}² + 1/B.

The azimuthal signal is 1 + c₂ cos 2(φ_t − φ_S) with two mechanisms summed:
a t-linear **deformation** term a₂ = −(P_zz/4)·ε_B0·B·\|t\| (exact only as
\|t\| → 0; default ε_B0 = −0.08, **and since 2026-09-04 that is ε_B0 = δ_{±1}/B = +2ΔB_{±1}/B = −1×ΔB₀/B** — ΔB_m being the cos 2Φ coefficient of the m state's slope, ΔB_m = δ_m/2, defined once at the declaration and previously used undefined, so the old label "ΔB₀/B" was off **by a sign**, not by a factor 2. The old band −(0.04…0.13) is a **deuteron** band; the measured ⁶Li one at this B is **−(0.0070…0.0527)** and the shipped default implies Q_charge(⁶Li) = **−0.9345 fm², 11.4× the measured** — see the caveat list and `phase_C_numbers.md` §C4) and a **flat gluon-transversity** term
amp·P_zz (default 0.01, band 3e-3…1e-2, the "exotic glue" scenario);
c₂ = 2·a₂(deformation) + amp·P_zz.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|

One genuinely new fact: **the slope is VM-independent** (1.5 % across ρ/φ/J/ψ), so `slope_b` needs no channel index. At Q² > 0.7 against `generate_inclusive`'s own σ_incl = 592 nb the all-VM fraction is **3.01e−02** — inside the {0.02, 0.08} band, but 86 % ρ⁰ (15.32 of 17.82 nb), i.e. events this channel deliberately excludes — while the part inside LiPolGen's own M_X window is **1.02e−03** (J/ψ alone), a **lower bound 40× below f0 = 0.04**, not a measurement. It is also unpolarized throughout (so it constrains neither `amp` nor `eps_b0`) and Gaussian, so it shows **no diffractive minimum at any A, at any energy**: a rate and slope baseline, never an imaging one. **Since 2026-09-04 those cross sections live in code**, as `estarlight_li6_coherent()` — the σ at eSTARlight's own window, the σ restricted to LiPolGen's Q² > 0.7, the measured-radius variants, the fitted slopes and the reconstructed decays' branchings, in one table on the same pattern as `mantysaari_a2_deuteron()`, so the rate arithmetic re-runs when an input moves instead of rotting in prose. **What that rate buys, priced in `validation/o5_a2_reach.py` (open item O5, `OPEN_ITEMS_SOLUTIONS.md` §11.3)**: at `Scenario::lumi_fb_per_nucleon` = 10 fb⁻¹/u — hence 10/6 = **1.667 fb⁻¹ of e+⁶Li**, the per-nucleon → per-nucleus division being where a factor 6 is easy to drop — J/ψ → e⁺e⁻ and arXiv:2511.05638's 17.75 % global efficiency give **3.13 × 10⁴** reconstructed coherent J/ψ, **4.37 × 10⁶** φ and **1.50 × 10⁸** ρ⁰; inside LiPolGen's own Q² > 0.7 window, 1.07 × 10⁴ / 2.75 × 10⁵ / 4.53 × 10⁶. ## 10. Polarized ⁶Li nucleon configurations for a dipole-model code

`cluster_config.hpp` is **opt-in and inert**: nothing in the generator calls it, it
adds no default, changes no existing number, and its output is a file. It samples
nucleon POSITIONS of a polarized ⁶Li in the α + d cluster picture so that an external
Good–Walker dipole-model code (`subnucleondiffraction`, the engine behind [Mant24])
can average a coherent amplitude over them — the missing input between §9's scenario
and a real polarized coherent-diffraction calculation for A > 2. Two entry points:
the C++ `ClusterConfigSampler`, and the `lipolgen-configs` console script, which is
deliberately a **separate** script rather than a subcommand of `lipolgen-run` —
adding subparsers there would have changed that parser's behaviour.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| What is sampled, and from which density | ⁶Li(1⁺) as a rigid α(0⁺) core plus a deuteron with relative orbital L = 0, 2 coupled to the deuteron spin S = 1. For radial functions normalised as ∫(f₀²+f₂²)x²dx = 1 the substate density is ρ_m(x, cos θ) = Σ_{m_S} \|Σ_L ⟨L (m−m_S) 1 m_S\|1 m⟩ f_L(x) Θ_L^{m−m_S}(cos θ)\|², **with no φ dependence**; `cluster_density` is that and `cluster_amp2` one m_S branch of it, both built from the library's own `clebsch_gordan` and `theta_lm` — no hand-expanded closed form lives in the library. Six nucleon positions come out per configuration: the α at −R/3 + s_i (four nucleons drawn from the ⁴He point density and recentred, with an optional hard core `--min-nn-separation`), the proton at +2R/3 + r/2 and the neutron at +2R/3 − r/2, total c.m. at the origin — Σ_i r⃗_i = 0 to **2.2e−15 fm** worst component over 20 000 configurations. Three α–d radial sources: `FitRescaled` (**default**: `li6.adr.fit` with its S and D waves rescaled by s₀ = 1.01281, s₂ = 0.89835 onto the overlap's own norms), `FitRaw` (P_D = 0.02542) and `OverlapRaw` (the `li6.ad` r block verbatim, noisy below ≈1.2 fm — its R₂ node sits at 1.1189 fm against the fit's 1.0648 fm, and that node is **real**, 3.3 σ, not a fit artefact, but decides nothing: r < 1.5 fm is −0.09 % of q_int. `FitRescaled` is the default by author decision; `OverlapRaw` moves Q_charge by +8.7 %, η by +11.5 %); the default gives P_D(α–d) = **0.02011**, rms R = 4.1187 fm, r_rms = 2.5386 fm. **The (−i)^L trap is not optional bookkeeping**: this module works in r space and applies NO phase, so its r-space S–D interference sign is OPPOSITE to the physical k-space one and to `VmcRadial`; the only observable check on the r-space signs is sign(Q) = sign(`LI6_QUADRUPOLE_FM2`), and neither convention may be "fixed" to match the other | [Mant24] Eqs. (7)–(8) — the density is those equations and is checked against them to 1e−14 — and the `subnucleondiffraction` code they are implemented in; the α and ⁶Li point densities are the ANL VMC `he4.density` / `li6.density` tables, fetched and md5-verified (`data/vmc/density/README.md`); the deuteron pair is `fdeut.av18` [AV18]. | Output format, sidecar and entry points | `write_snd_configs` writes the table plus a `path + ".meta.json"` sidecar, and **the sidecar is the authoritative record**. Row layout — fm, ion rest frame, c.m. at the origin, quantization axis already applied, `%.17g`, space separated, LF, **no header and no blank line**: `x1 y1 z1 … x6 y6 z6  t1 … t6  m` — 18 coordinates, then six isospins (+1 p, −1 n; ordering α, α, α, α, p, n), then the ion substate; fields 19–25 are past the 3A the consumer reads and are invisible to it, exactly as `he3.dat`'s own trailing fields are. `SndConfigFormat::He3Compatible` (**default**) is byte-compatible with that file because the upstream reader does a bare `ss >> x` per field and would **silently read garbage** from a `#` line; `Annotated` adds the header block for a patched reader, and that in-file block is the **pre-fill copy** — its `md5` and `git` stay null because there is no hash function and no JSON emitter anywhere in the C++ core, and `python/lipolgen/configs.py` fills them in the sidecar from hashlib and `git rev-parse HEAD`. The sidecar carries m, (θ_S, φ_S), seed/run, **every** option field, every input file with its size and its own printed normalization, `LIPOLGEN_VERSION`, and both the analytic and the sampled moments with their per-configuration standard deviations. Cost: **0.54 µs/config** in-process and **1.31 µs/config** through `lipolgen-configs` (0.131 s for 100 000; 1.06 s total wall including a 37 MB write and the md5) against a < 5 µs/config target, with a ≈0.25 s constructor (tables, nine (R, cos θ) CDFs, the recentred-core transform) paid once. ## 11. Tier T2: hadronization through a PYTHIA 8 `LHAup` bridge

PYTHIA's own hard-process machinery cannot take a per-event, Fermi-smeared
(k, p_N): with momentum spread `ProcessLevel` gates a new ECM on
`Beams:allowVariableEnergy`, which `Pythia::init` forbids for a hard process, so
the event would run at the initialisation √s; and even through an in-memory
`LHAup` at `Beams:frameType = 5` the total final-state four-momentum is forced to
the *initialisation* beam total by `BeamRemnants::setKinematics`. `BeamSetup`
additionally forces the beam on shell at its PDG mass, so a genuinely off-shell
bound nucleon is not representable directly either. The bridge therefore computes
the physical invariants q = k − k′, Q² = −q², W² = (q+p_N)² and builds a
**surrogate** e+N (or e+Pomeron) event on PYTHIA's own fixed beams matched to the
same (W², Q²):

    Ẽ′ = [P_A·k_B − B̃ + p_A Q²/(2E_B)] / (E_A + p_A) ,
    k̃_z = Q²/(2E_B) − Ẽ′ ,   k̃_T = √(Ẽ′² − k̃_z²) ,

with E_A = headroom × E_N,nominal (default headroom 1.5, a pure frame choice)
covering the ±20 % Fermi tail. Because the surrogate shares (W²,Q²) with the
physical event, mapping PYTHIA's hadronic system onto the physical one is a pure
Lorentz transformation, with a common momentum rescale λ solving
Σ√(m_i²+λ²|p_i|²) = W_want absorbing any residual mismatch. Two light-cone
fractions are kept apart: the **physical** ξ, which puts q + ξp_N on the mass
shell of the actual (moving, off-shell, or spacelike Pomeron) target,

    ξ = (Q²+m_q²) / [ (q·p_N) + √((q·p_N)² + p_N²(Q²+m_q²)) ] ,

and the **surrogate** ζ = (Q²+m_q²)/(P_A⁺ q̃⁻), which is what PYTHIA receives as
x₁; they differ only through p_N² ≠ m_A² and a dropped m_A² q̃⁺ term — sub-percent
at EIC energies.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| The `LHAup` surrogate at `Beams:frameType = 5` | As above. An event whose k̃_T² < 0 has no representable surrogate; `n_no_surrogate` counts three distinct failures (Ẽ′ ≤ 0 or k̃_T² < 0; q̃⁻ ≤ 0; the massless ζ outside (0,1)). Independently, W_sur < m_A + 0.15 GeV is refused and counted only in `n_failed` — W < 1.088 GeV on the nucleon beams, M_X < 0.15 GeV on the Pomeron one | [PYTHIA83] (version in use 8.317); `docs/surveys/pythia8_survey.md` §§4(v), 7; PYTHIA source pointers `ProcessLevel.cc:645`, `Pythia.cc:662`, `BeamRemnants.cc:662,935`, `BeamSetup.cc:645`, quoted at `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:20-40` | `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:404` `hadronize`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:370` `run`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:183` `headroom`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:337` `build`; `src/pythia/lhaup_dis.hpp:43` `LhaupDis`; `src/pythia/lhaup_dis.hpp:48` `setInit` |
| Frame map and momentum-rescale mass repair | A right-handed triad (e₃ = γ\* direction, e₁ = the incoming-lepton component ⊥ e₃, e₂ = e₃×e₁) is built in each rest frame; each hadron is boosted into PYTHIA's hadronic rest frame, re-expressed in the physical triad and boosted out. Because the rotation is linear and each rest-frame sum is zero, the transformed sum is exactly the physical hadronic system. The safeguarded-Newton rescale λ sits between the two boosts; every hadron mass and charge is left untouched. Measured worst \|λ−1\| ≈ 1.2e-13 on the nucleon instances (worst relative four-momentum deviation 8.4e-14 over 5000 events) and 6.7e-12 on the coherent instance after its compensation | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:219-230` (derivation), `:429-432` and `:145-146` (measurements) | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:98` `solve_rescale`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:72` `make_triad`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:69` `Triad`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:420` `last_rescale`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:347` `max_rescale_dev` |
| Physical ξ vs surrogate ζ, and the kinematic helpers | `dis_parton_fraction` returns the numerically stable root above, which reduces exactly to x_Bj for a massless on-shell target and stays the small positive root for p_N² < 0, and returns false when the discriminant is negative or ξ > 1. It is solved massless once as a representability pre-check and re-solved with the chosen quark's mass after `next()`. `dis_scattered_electron` gives the head-on-frame e′ of an inclusive event from (x,Q²,φ) for a collinear target | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:97-135` | `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:83` `dis_scattered_electron`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:149` `dis_scattered_electron`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:96` `dis_parton_fraction`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:171` `dis_parton_fraction`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:415` `last_xi_pythia`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:701` `dis_parton_fraction` |
| Struck-flavour sampling on PYTHIA's own beam PDF | P(q) ∝ e_q²·x f_q(ζ_q, max(Q², `q2_pdf_min`)) evaluated on PYTHIA's own beam PDF, so the flavour draw and PYTHIA's backward evolution read the same grid at the same x and scale. **Each flavour is weighted at its OWN ζ_q = (Q²+m_q²)/(P_A⁺q̃⁻)** (P2: previously all were weighted at the massless ζ, so a heavy flavour whose ζ_q had run past 1 stayed in the pool and burned a `next()` retry every draw; such flavours are now dropped before offering and counted). Offered: u,d,ū,d̄ always; s,s̄ if `include_strange` (default on); c,c̄ if `include_charm` (default on); b,b̄ if `include_bottom` (default off). m_q is the PYTHIA table mass for c/b and zero for u/d/s, matching `LesHouches:setQuarkMass = 1`'s policy so PYTHIA never silently reassigns m_c/m_b at the cost of moving the fixed scattered electron (measured 8.8 % of events, hadronic W shifted by up to 25 % before the fix). `q2_pdf_min` = 1.0 GeV² sits exactly at the default grid floor `SamplerGrid::q2_min` = 1.0 GeV² (`include/lipolgen/sampler.hpp:156` `q2_min`), so on a default run it clamps nothing; the scenario's looser 0.7 GeV² window edge never bites (§4). **No CLI twin exists** for any of these | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:137-161` | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:545` `zeta_of`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:568` `offer`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:186` `include_strange`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:187` `include_charm`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:188` `include_bottom`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:193` `q2_pdf_min`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:297` `n_flavour_dropped` |
| Fixed PYTHIA settings; the `PhaseSpace:*` silent cuts are bypassed | `configure` applies, in order: `Beams:frameType = 5`; `Check:beams = on` — **required, not off**: `BeamSetup::checkBeams` accepts a lepton+hadron pair only if a WeakBosonExchange flag is on, or `Check:beams` is on, or frameType == 4, so "off" makes `init()` fail; the four main341 DIS lines (`SpaceShower:dipoleRecoil = on`, `pTmaxMatch = 2`, `PDF:lepton = off`, `TimeShower:QEDshowerByL = off`); `SpaceShower:QEDshowerByQ = off` because a QED ISR photon off the struck quark recoils against the scattered lepton — the only other charge in the dipole — and moved the core generator's fixed e′ in 0.4 % of 500 events with W shifted by up to 25 % (final-state-quark photons stay ON: they belong to the hadronic system); `LesHouches:setLeptonMass = 0`; the random/print settings; `PDF:PomSet`/`PDF:PomRescale` on the Pomeron instance only; and finally the user's `settings`, which therefore win. Because the hard process arrives entirely through `LHAup` (strategy 3, `addProcess` with unit weight, no internal phase-space integration), **every `PhaseSpace:*` setting is irrelevant to this tier** — including the two silent cuts `PhaseSpace:pTHatMinDiverge` and `PhaseSpace:mHatMin` that a stock-PYTHIA DIS run has to discover and set. The hard phase space is the core generator's window, enforced upstream | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:280-347`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:32-36` | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:276` `configure`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:167` `settings`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:171` `verbosity`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:437` `applied_settings` |
| Three PYTHIA instances (proton, neutron, Pomeron beams) | The bridge owns up to three separate `Pythia` objects because the beam REMNANT, not just the PDF, depends on the beam id: striking a d quark leaves a uu diquark in a proton beam and a ud diquark in a neutron beam, while `PDF::resetValenceContent` sets beamType = −1 for id 2112 so `PDF::xf` swaps u↔d and ū↔d̄ automatically. The proton instance is always built; the neutron one unless `with_neutron_instance` is false; the Pomeron one unless `coherent_t2 == Off`. All three share one RNG stream. Both nucleon instances initialize in ≈0.34 s combined | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:172-181` | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:221` `Inst`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:228` `proton`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:234` `pomeron`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:337` `build`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:265` `inst_for`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:197` `with_neutron_instance`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:654` `--hadronize` |
| The LHA record, SPINUP, the retry budget and RNG routing | Four LHA lines per event: (1) the incoming quark, status −1, **first** because `ProcessContainer` assigns the first status −21 parton to beam A, exactly massless along +z, colour (101,0) for a quark and **(0,101) for an antiquark** — the sign-following orientation whose absence is a silent ≈50 % veto rate; (2) the incoming electron; (3) the outgoing electron; (4) the outgoing quark with mass m_q and the same colour tag, so the string runs struck quark ↔ beam remnant. `setIdX`/`setPdf` are filled; strategy 3 with unit weights makes PYTHIA's `sigmaGen` bookkeeping only — the cross section is the core generator's. The payload stays armed because `next()` may call `setEvent` more than once when the parton level is retried. SPINUP carries the electron helicity and the struck nucleon's polarization label, and is **consumed by nothing** (PYTHIA's DIS matrix element is helicity-averaged). On a veto the flavour is re-drawn from the same cumulative table and `next()` retried up to `max_retries` (default 4) more times, then the event fails. PYTHIA's randomness is routed through an `RndmEngine` backed by the caller's per-event `Rng`, so a given (seed, run, bunch, event) reproduces bit for bit at any thread count; `Random:seed` — set from `PythiaBridgeOptions::seed`, default **19780503** (`include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:163` `seed`) — only seeds the fallback stream used during `init()`.
