| Coherent tier: a γ\*–Pomeron beam (`Beams:idA = 990`) | A coherent event carrying `Role::Pomeron` (P_IP = P_ion − P_recoil) is hadronized by the identical surrogate + frame map on a third instance whose beam A is PYTHIA's own Pomeron. The identification is exact: p_N → P_IP (spacelike, P_IP² = t < 0); W² → M_X² = (q+P_IP)² (exact, because the recoil was solved against this identity); the beam-remnant diquark → a beam-remnant SINGLE ANTIQUARK, automatically colour- and charge-neutral against the struck quark. Because PYTHIA's m₀(990) = 0 the massless mass-shell solve gives ζ = Q²/(W²_sur+Q²), which equals β = Q²/(M_X²+Q²) up to the electron-mass compensation below (relative shift ≈2e-6; the docs' "verified to 10 digits" predates the compensation); each heavy flavour is still produced at its own ζ_q. This works because id 990 is a legal user beam even though PYTHIA's internal `Diffraction:doHard` cannot be driven externally and is structurally off for a virtual photon. `CoherentT2::Off` skips the third instance; `hadronize()` then returns false for a coherent event **and counts it in `n_failed`** (so in Off mode the failure count is the whole coherent sample, not a veto rate), leaving the record at T0 with X still final and conserving — turning the tier off costs fidelity, never conservation | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:462-498`; PYTHIA source pointers `BeamSetup.cc:869-875` (990 counts as a hadron) and `BeamParticle.cc:178` (meson-like beam handling) at `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:473-475`; design `docs/open_items/code_designs.md` §1(a′), prototype `docs/open_items/prototypes/pom_dis.cc` | `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:146` `CoherentT2`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:202` `coherent_t2`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:300` `n_pomeron`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3540` `make_coherent`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:656` `--coherent-t2`; `python/bindings.cpp:791` `t2_n_ok` |
| Pomeron flavour draw and its two silent traps | The flavour is drawn on PYTHIA's Pomeron PDF, controlled by `PDF:PomSet` (default **6 = H1 2006 Fit B LO**, PYTHIA's own default; the fifteen are 1 a Q²-independent toy, 2 π⁰ densities, 3/4/5 H1 NLO, 6 H1 LO, 7–10 ACTW NLO, 11 the Angantyr rescaled proton, 12/13 GKG18 LO and 14/15 GKG18 NLO — **6 is the only LO *H1* set, not "the only LO set", which is what this row said until 2026-09-04**: 12 and 13 are LO too) and `PDF:PomRescale` (default 1.0; it cancels out of the per-event-normalized draw and is exposed for reproducibility). At M_X = 5 GeV, Q² = 5 GeV² the Pomeron is 87 % gluon on set 6, so the flavour choice is by construction sea/gluon-dominated and set-sensitive. **Trap 1**: the colour-tag orientation must follow the initiator's sign. **Trap 2**: at small β the LO Pomeron grid has literally no quarks (gluon momentum fraction 1.000) until Q² ≈ 1.5–1.75 GeV², below the default `q2_pdf_min` = 1.0 clamp, so a default coherent run takes a fallback on ≈20 % of events (measured 86/400 at config 1). When every weight is zero the sampler falls back to bare charge weights e_q² over **LIGHT flavours only** — all three H1 2006 grids carry no charm or bottom at any (β,Q²), Fit A NLO (3) and Fit B NLO (4) exactly as much as Fit B LO (6), because all three are PYTHIA's one `PomH1FitAB` class and its `xfUpdate` assigns `xc = xcbar = xb = xbbar = 0.` unconditionally (`PartonDistributions.cc:2630`; the "H1 **LO** grids" wording this row carried until 2026-09-04 was true but too narrow) — and a previous version wrongly reused the DIS offer list and put ≈7 % of a default coherent sample on zero-PDF-support charm. A non-zero fallback count is ROUTINE, and since 2026-09-04 it is **visible**: printed at the run banner and recorded as `meta["n_pom_flavour_fallback"]` / `meta["pom_flavour_fallback_frac"]`. **It is not a systematic — measured, it costs exactly zero**: raising `q2_pdf_min` from 1.0 to 1.75 takes the share from 20.70 % to 0.00 % (set 6), 31.25 % → 0.00 % (3), 19.45 % → 2.65 % (12), 20.10 % → 3.23 % (13), 21.65 % → 5.12 % (15) — and **35.52 % → 29.32 % on set 4, the one set where it does NOT collapse**, an exception that must travel with the sentence — and leaves a **bit-identical** final state in all six cases, set 4 included (which is why its residual 29 % costs nothing either) (`pid` and `p4`, ~34 000 hadrons over 4000 events), because every Pomeron DPDF carries a single light-quark singlet so e_q²·xf_q ∝ e_q² exactly over the light flavours and the "fallback" *is* the true draw. This row's earlier remedy — "removes it at the cost of clamping every flavour weight to that Q²" — was true about the counter and **false about the cost**; the one real cost appears only at `q2_pdf_min` = 3.0, on the GKG18 sets, and is a **charm** effect (bit-identical again with `include_charm = false`). This PDF-set choice is the tier's largest systematic, and it has been SCANNED (2026-09-04): the T0 columns are bit-identical across the **fourteen** sets the bridge constructor still admits (1–10, 12–15 — one md5 `ffd35a3a62b591c547e9ca2ac4301b5d`, re-measured 2026-09-05; set 11 gave the same md5 in the original scan but is refused now, so fourteen is the count that reproduces and this row said "all fifteen" until 2026-09-05), so the band on \|t\|, x_P, M_X and σ is *identically zero* and the systematic lives on the hadronic final state — ⟨n_charged⟩ **−2.6 %/+9.9 %** and the **kaon fraction a factor 2.8** over the twelve DPDF fits (3–10, 12–15); set 11 is refused because 100.00 % of its events take the fallback (`docs/OPEN_ITEMS_SOLUTIONS.md` §5.1) | [H1FitB]; `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:500-561` | `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:264` `pom_set`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:271` `pom_rescale`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:341` `n_pom_flavour_fallback`; `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:634` `n_pom_flavour_fallback`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:662` `--pom-set`; `python/lipolgen/cli.py:688` `--pom-rescale` |
| Electron-beam-mass bookkeeping and the compensated surrogate | PYTHIA reconstructs beam B (the electron) at its physical mass even though the surrogate hands it over massless (`LesHouches:setLeptonMass = 0` only keeps the OUTGOING lepton massless). A baryon beam A absorbs this in its composite remnant, but the meson-like Pomeron beam — a single-antiquark remnant with no longitudinal freedom — closes the record on the lepton side's massive light-cone minus, so every event is short by Δ(E−p_z) = −m_e²/(2E_e), i.e. Δ(m²) = −(m_e²/(2E_e))(2E_A − Q²/(2E_e)) on the hadronic system, with E_A the **surrogate** beam-A energy (headroom × √(p_u²+m_p²) = 149.3 GeV at config 1). Measured −3.896e-6 GeV², constant to 0.15 % over 300 events and matching the formula to four significant digits. Harmless for global conservation (the rescale repairs it) but amplified near the 1.2 GeV M_X floor to \|λ−1\| ≈ 6e-6. The fix: on the id-990 branch the surrogate is built at the COMPENSATED target W²_sur = M_X² + \|Δ(m²)\| while the rescale still targets the physical M_X, bringing the coherent worst \|λ−1\| to 6.7e-12. (Rejected alternative: setting particle 11's m₀ = 0 in the Pomeron instance, which would also touch every e⁺e⁻ Dalitz decay in the hadronic system) | `docs/PYTHIA_BRIDGE.md:589-621` | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:507` `w2_sur`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:183` `headroom`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:347` `max_rescale_dev` |
| Record write-back: spectator preservation and `HadronicX` demotion | After the frame map the bridge writes `Role::Hadron`/`Status::Final` particles for every PYTHIA final-state particle **except the scattered electron** (identified as the bottom copy of the status-23 outgoing lepton) — so FSR photons, Dalitz e⁺e⁻, decay muons and neutrinos are `Role::Hadron` too and are counted by the HFS summary. Any pre-existing `Role::HadronicX` pseudo-particle is demoted from Final to Intermediate so a status-1 sum is never double-counted. When the target was implicit (the deprecated cluster branch or the inclusive fallback — **not** the coherent branch, which already names its target) the nucleon actually used is appended as an Intermediate `Role::StruckNucleon` with charge by pdg and pol = 9 (unknown). `Role::Spectator`, `Role::PartnerSpectator` and `Role::IntactRecoil` are **never read and never touched** — no recoil correction, the BeAGLE light-nucleus rule — and are verified bit-identical before and after `hadronize()` | [BeAGLE22] §II and `docs/surveys/beagle_survey.md:120-127`; write-back table `docs/T2_CHAIN.md:41-45` | `src/pythia/pythia_bridge.cpp:803` `HadronicX`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:407` `last_hadrons`; `include/lipolgen/pythia_bridge.hpp:411` `last_struck_nucleon` |

## 12. Pipeline, output and the command line

One `Pipeline` composes the DIS sampler, the generator, the tagged and coherent
modules into the five run channels. Every event is a **pure function of its
global index** — `Rng(seed, run, category, local counter)` — so `event(i)` is
thread-safe and `for_each(sink, nthreads)` emits exactly the same events in
exactly the same order for any thread count.

| Aspect / model | Approach (formulas, defaults, options) | References | Implementing symbols |
|---|---|---|---|
| Channel dispatch and the hadronic-X balance | Each channel closes its own exact four-momentum identity with one pseudo-particle X (pdg 92, `Role::HadronicX`, always written to HepMC3 as status 3 while carrying `Status::Final` in the `Event` record so `total_final()`/`momentum_residual()` include it). Three free functions state that balance for any channel and are the "checks you get for free" of `docs/USAGE.md:2014-2020`: `momentum_residual` (the residual four-vector, zero to rounding), `momentum_scale` (what ENTERED the balance, for a relative comparison) and `charge_residual` (exactly zero). `add_hadronic_x` **throws** if X comes out spacelike instead of clipping it massless — an invariant of the channel's balance, not something to patch. The redraw loop exists only in `make_tagged` (up to 64 tries, testing the finished T0/T1 X on the event's own stream); `make_coherent` has no redraw, its X being timelike by construction of the x_P model plus the constructor's per-cell C1 test | `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:13-39`; `docs/CONVENTIONS.md:312-314` | `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:244` `PipelineChannel`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:251` `pipeline_channel_name`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:306` `pipeline_channel_name`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3368` `add_hadronic_x`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3396` `make_inclusive`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:3406` `make_tagged`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:30` `kTaggedMaxRedraw`; `include/lipolgen/event.hpp:33` `HadronicX`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:1308` `momentum_residual`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:1310` `momentum_scale`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:1312` `charge_residual` |
| Fidelity tier switch | `Tier::T1` (default on tagged channels) resolves the struck cluster; `Tier::T0` leaves it as one off-shell pseudo-particle and is kept only for bit-compatibility with pre-T1 numbers. The switch is a no-op on Inclusive and Coherent, which have no struck cluster. The constructor overwrites `BreakupOptions::beta` with the run's `cluster_beta` unconditionally and fills `BreakupOptions::f2` only when the caller left it null; `p_d` (0.045), `kappa_nn`, the deuteron model's `nk` × `nc` = 280 × 96 grid and the triton step's `n_grid` = 4096 (`include/lipolgen/breakup.hpp:234` `n_grid`) travel from `PipelineConfig::breakup` verbatim. It also **overwrites `StruckClusterOptions::scenario` and `::grid` unconditionally** with `PipelineConfig::scenario` / `::grid` (`src/core/pipeline.cpp:1143` `scenario`; `src/core/pipeline.cpp:1531` `grid`), so those two fields of `PipelineConfig::struck` (`include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:194` `scenario`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:196` `grid`) are DEAD on the pipeline path — only `struck.delta_func` and `struck.with_perp` reach the struck-cluster sampler. **No CLI or config-file key sets the tier** — it is a `PipelineConfig` field reachable from C++ and the Python bindings | `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:287-299` | `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:301` `Tier`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:847` `tier`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:852` `breakup`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:852` `breakup`; `include/lipolgen/pipeline.hpp:847` `tier` |
| Far-forward optics choice and route labelling | `OpticsChoice` selects the envelope used to LABEL a spectator's or recoil's route: the two Yellow-Report choices call `yr_optics`, `Tagging`/`TaggingLegacyLevers` the tabulated lithium working points (§5), `Custom` uses `PipelineConfig::optics` verbatim. The route itself is **recomputed, never stored**, from the finished event: R = (\|p_frag\|/Z_frag)/(\|p_beam\|/Z_beam), θ = atan2(p_T,p_z), φ = atan2(p_y,p_x), with `rp_tagged` applying the Roman-Pot main-window + near-beam mask on top. A neutral fragment is routed by angle alone with no rigidity; an event with neither a spectator nor an intact recoil (i.e. the Inclusive channel) always routes "lost". **Whether the three routing knobs (`optics`, `n_sigma`, `pot_config`) are READ on a given run is MEASURED, not asserted**: `Pipeline::route_reach()` draws the sample once and re-routes it through `route_probe_event` under every other tabulated envelope, every other `n_sigma` and every other machine configuration, stopping as soon as all three have been seen to move a label. **The answer is a property of the SAMPLE, not of the channel** (measured 2026-09-05 at 400 events, seed 4242, the shipped defaults): on `tagged-6Li-alpha` both `optics` and `pot_config` come back **read**, on `coherent` `optics` is read and `pot_config` is not (an intact recoil is never over-rigid), and on `tagged-d-p` — whose route column is 369 lost / 17 + 13 + 1 elsewhere at that size — **neither** is. The SEED is half of that statement: the same `tagged-d-p` channel has `optics` **read** at 60 events with seed 7 and not-read at 2000 with seed 11, so a tabulated per-channel rule would be wrong in one column or the other and the probe is what stands in its place. **Since 2026-09-05 the not-read label on a channel that HAS a route still NAMES the envelope** — `not read on this run at 10x100 high-acceptance (the classifier IS consulted here; this run's route labels are insensitive to yr-high-divergence)` — because that branch is the one where the classifier *is* consulted and only the SENSITIVITY is absent; the bare "not read on this run" it printed before said the stronger thing and dropped the envelope name that `meta["optics"]`, `meta["pot_config"]`, the three rows of `meta["knob_provenance"]` and the CLI banner header line all take from this one label. (`n_sigma` has a provenance row and no scalar `meta` key of its own.) | Installable Python package (`pip install`), beside the in-tree build | `pyproject.toml` makes the repository a **scikit-build-core** project, so `LIPOLGEN_DEPS_PREFIX=… pip install -e .` builds the C++ core, the pybind11 extension and LiPolGen's own bridge libraries straight into a venv with **no `env.sh` and no `build/` directory**; the in-tree `cmake -S . -B build` flow is untouched and the two never collide, because the wheel build uses its own `.skbuild/{wheel_tag}` cache. The version is **not retyped**: scikit-build-core reads it out of `CMakeLists.txt`'s `project(LiPolGen VERSION …)` by regex, so `CMakeLists.txt` stays the single source of truth. Two console entry points are installed: **`lipolgen-run`** (`lipolgen.cli:main`, the same thing the in-tree `build/lipolgen-run` shim runs) and **`lipolgen-configs`** (`lipolgen.configs:main`, §10). Editable installs use `redirect` mode, which redirects **only the pure-Python half** back to `python/lipolgen`: a `.py` edit takes effect with no reinstall, while **the compiled half goes stale after a C++ edit** and needs `pip install -e .` again (`rebuild` stays off, because ninja/cmake are only guaranteed inside the build isolation environment). Extras: `hepmc` (pyhepmc, for reading the HepMC3 files back), `yaml` (needed by `--config-file` alone), `test`. C++ tests are skipped in a wheel build (`-DLIPOLGEN_BUILD_TESTS=OFF`). Measured: fresh-venv editable install **66 s** (2026-09-02); `pip wheel . --no-deps` **87.77 s → 1 779 767 B** (2026-09-05; the `data/vmc` tables are small enough to vendor), the in-tree flow unchanged. `PYTHIA8DATA` / `LHAPDF_DATA_PATH` still need exporting at run time, and **the wheel is not relocatable as built** — its RPATH is `$ORIGIN` plus *this machine's* deps prefix. **`auditwheel repair` has now been RUN, not merely recommended** (2026-09-05, auditwheel 6.8.2 driving patchelf 0.19.1): **5.35 s**, vendoring `libpythia8.so` (14.0 MB), `libLHAPDF.so` (1.24 MB) and `libHepMC3.so.4` (1.18 MB) into a `lipolgen.libs/` directory and rewriting four RPATHs to `$ORIGIN:$ORIGIN/../lipolgen.libs`, for **7 232 971 B** under the tag **`manylinux_2_35_x86_64`** (glibc 2.35 — a wider tag needs a build inside an older manylinux image, which is not done here). Verified with the entire deps prefix masked out of the filesystem: `import lipolgen`, all three tiers True, and the pure-C++ generator — including the wheel's own vendored VMC tables — **work**; `LhapdfSF` and `PythiaBridge` **fail** (`Couldn't find required lhapdf.conf system config file`; `PYTHIA Error in Settings::init: settings file <build-machine path>/Index.xml not found`), because the two libraries' DATA trees are not in the wheel and their compiled-in defaults are the build machine's absolute paths — so `PYTHIA8DATA` / `LHAPDF_DATA_PATH` / `LIPOLGEN_PYTHIA8_PDFDATA` still have to be exported, and with them pointed at those trees on any other path the T2 HepMC3 output is byte-identical. Vendoring those three `.so`s makes whoever redistributes the result a redistributor of the combined **GPL-3** work; nothing has been published, and the decision is a row in the 2026-09-03 author-decision table | `docs/USAGE.md:11-66`; the settings themselves live in `pyproject.toml` (build backend and `[project]`, lines 1–28; `[project.scripts]`, 30–36; `[tool.scikit-build]` and `wheel.packages`, 38–49; `build-dir`, 51–58; `[tool.scikit-build.editable]` `mode = "redirect"`, 59–75; the version provider, 76–79) and in `CMakeLists.txt`'s `if(SKBUILD)` blocks (the RPATH block, lines 30–55, and the install rules, **279–323**) — **neither file is inside the checker's path set**, so those two line ranges are read, not machine-verified, and the second one had drifted: it was recorded here as 240–282 and was 248–292 even before 2026-09-05's tier-aware examples loop moved it again, both re-read by hand on 2026-09-05; the measured packaging run is `docs/PACKAGING.md`; the README paragraph was applied to `README.md` in the 2026-09-03 close-out (the snippet is kept in `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-02/README_packaging_snippet.md`) | `python/lipolgen/cli.py:1094` `main`; `python/lipolgen/configs.py:332` `main`; `python/lipolgen/__init__.py:423` `make_config` |

---

## 13. Not handled (explicitly)

**Spin formalism**

- The rank-3 (octupole) moment exists only for j = 3/2 and throws for any other
  spin; `tensor_polarization` and `moments_along_axis` throw outside j ∈ {1, 3/2}.
  No general-J tensor/octupole formula is provided.
- `populations_maxent` requires strictly \|P_z\| < 1 and cannot represent a fully
  polarized fill; `rho_from_populations` throws on populations that are negative
  beyond −1e-12 or do not sum to 1 within 1e-9 — no soft renormalization.
- There is no CLI or config-file knob for the ⁷Li rank-3 moment O. Its rank-2
  moment T **has had one since 2026-09-06**: `--pzz-mode typed` makes
  `helicity_flip_plan` set `use_explicit_pzz` and honour `--pzz`. At the
  DEFAULT `--pzz-mode ladder` T is still the spin-temperature value implied by
  `--pz` (see §1), so `--plan helicity-flip --pzz 0.6` gives T = 0.4 at
  P_z = 0.7 — and **since 2026-09-04 the run banner prints the fill's own
  recorded moments on every run**, so that substitution is not silent. The
  typed mode REFUSES a T outside the plan's domain rather than clamping to it
  (0.26 ≤ T ≤ 0.58 at P_z = 0.7, so the CLI's own default 0.6 is refused there
  by 0.02); no fill was moved by default, and the cost of the typed one is
  measured in `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-06/phase_A_numbers.md` §A3
  (`OPEN_ITEMS_SOLUTIONS.md` §15, decision D13).
- **Run-plan spin vs ion spin is now validated on the inclusive branch too**
  (2026-09-04). It used to be checked for tagged channels alone, and this
  document claimed `--isotope 7Li --channel inclusive --plan transverse-tensor`
  "silently runs three spin-1 categories on a ⁷Li beam"; **measured, that was
  wrong twice over** — `transverse_tensor_plan` builds **one** category, and
  the run did not proceed silently but threw two frames down out of
  `InclusiveKernel::amplitudes` ("spin state J = 1.000000 is not the kernel's
  ion spin 1.500000"), naming neither the plan nor the fix. `make_plan` now
  refuses all three spin-1 plans at J ≠ 1, and the `Pipeline` constructor makes
  the same spin check on the inclusive branch that the tagged branch always
  made (`phase_D_numbers.md` §D1.4).
- `beams.hpp` covers five species (p, d, ³He, ⁶Li, ⁷Li); any other name throws,
  as does `nucleus_mass` for any (name, A, Z) outside its fixed table.
- The ⟨P₂⟩ = −T/5 polarimeter identity is stated and tested only for the ⁷Li
  tagged α channel; it is neither derived nor claimed for ⁶Li or any other J.
- The rank-3 (octupole) sector never reaches the cross section, and §1 now says
  why that is exact rather than convenient: `g1_rank3` multiplies the beam
  helicity only. The flip side is **not** handled — A∥ on a J = 3/2 target is
  λ_e-odd, so it *does* pick up R₃, the default spin-temperature ⁷Li fill carries
  R₃ ≠ 0, and no rank-3 structure function is implemented to price that.

**Inclusive kernel and structure functions**

- The exact finite-γ tensor kernel is **off by default**: the O(γ²) rate-sector
  leakage into cos 2φ (Δ_fake/(γ²b₁) ≈ 0.14–0.16) is carried as a systematic of
  the Δ extraction, never subtracted automatically, and depends on the unmeasured
  b₃/b₄ slots.
- Δ (gluonometry) keeps its massless kinematic factor even on the `tensor_gamma`
  path — it is outside Cosyn's b₁–b₄ basis and is given no finite-γ treatment.
- A⊥ is never switched on by the pipeline (`with_perp` is false on both the
  inclusive and the tagged path, and no CLI or config-file key exposes it); when
  it is on it uses the massless ε(y) and D(y) irrespective of `target_mass`.
- The finite-γ kinematics use the FREE nucleon mass, never
  `Ion::mass_per_nucleon`; the bound mass would move γ² by ≈1 % of an already
  ≤10 % correction.
- No CLI flag exposes `target_mass`, `tensor_gamma`, `g2_mode` or `g2_scale`;
  they are set only through `Options` in code, tests or scripts.
- Rank-2 structure is gated to ion spin 1 or 3/2: `tables()` fills b₁/b₂/Δ for
  those two spins alone and the rank-2 branch runs only when both the kernel's
  and the state's spin are ≥ 1; below spin 1 the whole tensor and Δ sector is
  exactly zero. b₃/b₄ are filled for every spin but read only on the
  `tensor_gamma` path.
- The polarized medium ratio (`cbt_`/`tmt_polarized_emc_ratio`) is **not
  reachable from event generation at all**: no kernel passes a `medium_ratio` to
  `g1_nucleus` and `Options` has no polarized-medium slot. Likewise the
  unpolarized `emc_ratio` hook is empty in every default kernel, so F₂A carries
  no medium modification anywhere.
- On the tagged channels the struck-cluster kernel takes F₂ and g₁ from
  `--unpol-sf`/`--pol-sf` (`StruckClusterOptions::f2_source`/`g1_model`, added
  2026-09-04 — before that it had no structure-function slot at all, and
  `PipelineConfig::kernel` never reached it either), but R and the EMC hook
  there are still hard-locked to `r_sigma_lt`/none — the EMC hook on every
  channel, R on every channel but the inclusive ⁶Li `li6-convolution` path,
  which `--r-source` opened on 2026-09-06 and where alone it is accepted. **`--b1-model` does not reach them**: it is refused outside the
  inclusive channel, so on a tagged channel nothing selects the b₁ camp
  either. Nowhere does the CLI select the b₁ mode (digitized vs toy), the Δ
  interpretation, the Δ variant/dilution/α_s or the EMC
  baseline; the R backend is selectable on exactly one path (`--r-source`,
  ⁶Li `li6-convolution`) and nowhere else.
- **No published ⁶Li b₁ measurement exists, and nothing beyond the α–d picture is
  modelled.** The default `Li6B1` embedded-deuteron rank-2 transfer is explicitly
  an inference; the opt-in four-term convolution of §3 replaces the constant with
  a light-cone convolution but is still **entirely an α + d calculation** — the
  18 % of ⁶Li that is not α + d is given b₁ = 0 by default, the α's own EMC effect
  is not modelled, and there is no non-cluster (shell-model, GFMC, lattice)
  ⁶Li b₁ anywhere in the repository to check it against. Its **A = 2 magnitude
  gate now passes for one unpolarised nucleon input** (ratio 0.843 with
  MSTW2008 LO at CDKS Eq. (21) — `--b1-unpol mstw`; **0.440 on the shipped
  `toy` default, outside the window**; 1.000338 with CD-Bonn as well, a residual
  below the error of digitizing a published figure rather than three-digit
  agreement with CDKS; §3), which lifted the publication ban but does **not** make
  an A = 6 number validated — nothing checks the α–d step itself, which is why
  the ±100 % band is mandatory. Whether Δ should carry an analogous rank-2
  transfer (rather than the flat 1/3 dilution) is open.
- The Close–Kumano sum rule ∫b₁ dx = 0 is **reported, not enforced**, and only
  over the digitized range of the raw column — which is per DEUTERON for Miller
  and per NUCLEON for CDKS, so `close_kumano_integral(false)` and
  `close_kumano_integral(true)` are not on the same scale and must never be
  compared to each other.
- Both polarized-EMC curves are for nuclei other than the target and need a
  valence-scale transfer whose baseline is a stated CHOICE, not a computed fact.
- `LhapdfG1` (NNPDFpol1.1) carries three flavours only; Δc = Δb = 0 is the PDF
  set's own convention and cannot be turned on here. The production α_s
  convention (the CT18NLO table) has no C++ equivalent in the core library.
- LHAPDF-backed backends require the optional LHAPDF build, and the nuclear
  F₂A/g₁A combinations are not duplicated for them — a caller must wrap them in
  the existing `NuclearF2` / `g1_nucleus`.

**Sampling and estimators**

- Modulation amplitudes are evaluated only at (x,Q²) **cell centres** — adequate
  for binned Phase-1 estimators by construction, not for unbinned in-cell shapes.
- `cos2phi_moment` is valid for uniform acceptance only; the binned fit requires
  uniform bins and a C(1 + A cos 2φ′) shape (a residual cos φ′ term is orthogonal
  only at full coverage) and needs ≥ 3 live bins after the acceptance probe.
- The (A−1) nuclear remnant is deliberately **not** written at T0's per-nucleon
  balance; the tagged tier owns it, and that is what turns the per-nucleon
  balance into a whole-nucleus one.
- The 25 mrad crossing angle is a LAB transform applied downstream, never inside
  the T0 assembly.
- The polarimetry-scale systematic `delta_p_over_p` has no CLI option; every CLI
  run carries measured ≡ true polarizations. `--rel-lumi-offset` is silently
  ignored by the single-category transverse-tensor plan.

**Tagged channels and breakup**

- Coherences between struck-cluster spin states are dropped: p(m_S\|M,k,k̂) is a
  DIAGONAL truncation of the density matrix.
- No partial wave beyond L = 2 is supported anywhere (`theta_lm` and the analytic
  radial switch both throw).
- VMC tables return exactly 0 outside their tabulated k range (no extrapolation),
  and sign information above ≈3 fm⁻¹ is deliberately ignored (Monte-Carlo noise
  floor).
- The deuteron control channel **defaults to Hulthén**, and since 2026-09-04
  `--cluster-wave vmc` reaches it (open item C5.4): it was a wiring choice and
  not a data gap, `data/vmc/deuteron/fdeut.av18`'s u(k)/w(k) *are* the p–n
  relative S and D waves, and the flag used to be **silently ignored** there.
  `VmcAV18` now selects that table at its own P_D = **0.057600**
  (`deuteron_av18_p_d()`, +28 % over the scenario `P_D_DEUTERON` = 0.045), worth
  **−2.03 %** on the channel's `vector_dilution` and **−1.18 %** on its
  `tensor_dilution`. The relative S–D sign does **not** flip (CDKS's φ₂ = −W
  with φ_L = i^L ψ_L gives ψ₂ = +W, the **stored** convention the
  positive-definite Hulthén forms already assume) — the opposite of the ⁶Li α–d
  case. **That does not license summing ψ₂ into the amplitude, and until
  2026-09-06 the code did (corrected here on that date):** `build_amp2`
  consumes φ_L = i^L ψ_L, i.e. φ₂ = i²ψ₂ = **−W**, and applied no phase at all,
  so the tagged S–D interference sign was inverted against Cosyn–Weiss II
  Eq. (6.12) (`src/core/tagged.cpp` `build_amp2`;
  `docs/benchmarking/07_cw_sign_investigation.md`). `rad_[2]` and `Wave::psi()`
  are not negated, so ψ₂ = +W stays the stored convention. Hulthén stays the
  default: it is the analytic pair the ⁶Li channel it controls is built from,
  and `fdeut.av18` prints no MC errors, so the AV18 row carries no band.
- **The ⁶Li α tag's EMBEDDED deuteron follows the same flag** (open item C5.5b,
  2026-09-04). It is read in two places besides the α–d relative motion:
  `TaggedChannel::dis_target` (the struck cluster's g₁, through
  `InclusiveKernel::g1a` → `PolSF::g1_nucleus`) and `BreakupOptions` (the T1
  struck-nucleon **momentum and** spin draw — `ClusterBreakup` samples k from
  the same deuteron). Until 2026-09-04 **neither** followed the flag —
  both stayed on the scenario Hulthén P_D = 0.045 while the relative motion
  moved to the VMC overlap — so every polarized tagged-α observable was
  **+2.069 %** high against the AV18 deuteron of that same Hamiltonian.
  `VmcAV18` now puts `DEUTERON_AV18()` on `dis_target` (0.913600 against the
  scenario 0.9325) and `BreakupOptions::source` on the breakup, worth
  **−2.027 %** on that opt-in path and nothing at all on the Hulthén default.
  ⁷Li deliberately does not move: this tree has no AV18 A = 3 wave function, so
  the flag selects the α–t relative motion alone there, and the d control's
  struck object is a free neutron on either setting.
- Under `--cluster-wave vmc` the tagged α–d D-state probability becomes 0.0193
  while the **inclusive** effective polarization keeps 0.0867 — in that mode the
  two genuinely differ, by **+11.61 %** (vector) and **+6.58 %** (rank-2), and
  §3's dilution row records why substituting the VMC value is the wrong fix.
  Since 2026-09-04 `PipelineConfig::validate` **refuses** `cluster_wave` on
  `inclusive` and `coherent`, the two channels that never read it: accepting it
  unread was the route by which that 11.61 % reached a user who believed they
  had asked for a VMC ⁶Li row. `cluster_vmc_mc_sigma`, a 0.02 % effect, was
  already refused the same way.
  Independently, `--p-d` never reaches `LI6().eff_pol`.
- The ANL Monte Carlo errors are **carried since 2026-09-04** (open item C5.2)
  and were parsed-then-dropped before it: `VmcRadial::dpsi()` holds the files'
  own 1σ, `shifted_by_sigma` and `norm2_error` expose the band, and
  `vmc_mc_sigma` / `PipelineConfig::cluster_vmc_mc_sigma` is the knob (0 =
  today bit for bit; refused wherever no error column exists). Measured, it is
  **0.47 % / 1.62 %** on the S/D norms, 1.1 % on P_D per σ and **0.02 %** on the
  tagged tensor observable — negligible against the 6.6 % wave-function choice.
- ³H → p + nn is explicitly not modelled as a VMC overlap: it is a genuine
  three-body breakup, and no such spectroscopic-overlap file exists at the source.
- Neither triton branch has any D-wave/tensor structure (both isotropic in the
  relative direction); the spectral-function branch factorizes S_N(k,E) into n(k)
  times a k-independent pair excitation density, which a real Faddeev/AV18
  S(k,E) would not. The CS parameterization gives no minority-nucleon (struck
  proton) distribution at all — the isoscalar approximation used instead is
  acknowledged to be wrong in the isovector regime.
- `cluster_species()` throws for any (Z,A) outside nucleon, deuteron and triton;
  no other cluster species is handled.
- The α_s light-front variable is the MINUS fraction, with the opposite k_z sign
  to the Strikman–Weiss/Cosyn–Weiss convention (§5) — an open bookkeeping item.
- The struck-cluster off-shell / spectator-on-shell split is a **choice**, not a
  derived result, and FSI never modifies it.

**FSI**

- FSI is restricted to the three tagged channels; `validate()` throws for
  `Inclusive` and `CoherentLi6`.
- The principal-value (off-shell) piece of the longitudinal rescattering integral
  is dropped, not computed.
- The weight is spin-independent by construction: the model carries **no spin
  dependence of the rescattering**, which the cited reference lists as an open
  question. Quote it as an unpolarized-shape systematic, never as a correction to
  A_zz.
- `GlauberNucleon` is not a true correlated per-nucleon product: the
  centre-of-mass constraint Σ_i s_i = 0 and short-range NN correlations in the
  cluster density are an explicit open TODO.
- The formation ramp is unreachable from `cli.py`/`PipelineConfig` (only the
  single fixed σ_XN row is), as are ε, B_XN, `elastic_gain` and every grid field.
- `cluster_point_a2_fm2` recognizes a fixed table of clusters and throws
  otherwise; `φ_k` is carried but unused (the eikonal kernel is azimuthally
  symmetric).

**Radiative corrections**

- **The exact Mo–Tsai elastic radiative tail is not implemented and was not even
  obtained for this run.** What ships instead is a **BAND OF TWO TAIL MODELS**,
  and neither edge is "the" radiative tail. `RcTailModel::TPeak` (the default,
  bit for bit every published number) is POLRAD's **t-peak alone** and is a
  LOWER BOUND on the dilution. `RcTailModel::TPeakPlusLL` (`--rc-tail-model
  t-peak+ll`, added 2026-09-03) adds the leading-log s- and p-peaks — the same
  construction that used to live only in the test binary, promoted to
  `ll_peaks_spin1` / `ll_peaks_qe` in `src/core/rc.cpp` so that T8(c) gates the
  shipped code path.
  **The upper edge is a STATED MODEL, not a controlled O(α) expansion**: it sums
  an η_A quadrature and a single-z collinear leading log, so it is good to the
  worse of the two (~5–10 %), and its radiator carries an uncancelled soft
  `1/(1−z)` that overshoots as `y → 0`. **Neither edge carries a tensor s/p
  peak** — POLRAD supplies none and none is invented here — so the s+p enter the
  **unpolarised** numerator only and `TPeakPlusLL` **lowers the tensor fraction**
  of the tail (`r_T/r_U` ×0.66139 / ×0.0031829 / ×6.6076e−05 at x = 0.01 / 0.10 /
  0.30, Q² = 5). Since 2026-09-06 that fraction is **bounded, not computed** —
  `--rc-sp-tensor-scale` (default 0, refused unless `--rc-tail-model
  t-peak+ll`) prices it with the elastic t-peak's own tensor fraction — but the
  bound covers the **elastic** s/p column only and is **empty at those three
  points** (bit-identical: ⁶Li's coherent form factor is 45+ decades down at the
  s/p vertex Q′² ≈ Q²), recovering **0.0 %** of the collapse there and at most
  **21.72 %** anywhere on the grid; the **quasi-elastic** s/p column, which is
  the whole of the collapse at x ≥ 0.10, is bounded by **nothing**.
  Measured, and the event-weighted and per-cell statements are **different**:
  for ⁶Li at Q² ≥ 20 GeV² and y ≤ 0.9 the two edges agree to **+0.62 %** on the
  *event-weighted* mean dilution (8.719649e−03 → 8.773752e−03 over **5194** of
  200 000 events, seed 1234, **at the CLI's default fill P_z = 0.7**; at
  **P_z = 0** the same build gives 8.489138e−03 → 8.540716e−03 over **5182**,
  **+0.61 %**. *(Re-measured at both P_z, 2026-09-15. The note this line
  carried from 2026-09-06 — that the 5182 / 8.48914e−03 → 8.54072e−03
  absolutes "no longer reproduce" because that run's Phase A had moved the ⁶Li
  kernel normalisation and with it the sampler's cell weights — is
  **WITHDRAWN**: those digits reproduce exactly on this build at P_z = 0,
  which is the fill they were published on; the 2026-09-06 re-measurement had
  switched to the CLI default P_z = 0.7 without saying so. Phase A moved
  nothing here.)* But
  **331 of the 1356 accepted cells in that same
  window — 24.4 %, 28.2 % of its cross section — differ by more than 1 %**,
  worst **×6444** at x = 0.7943, y = 0.0088, Q² = 27.8, with **318 of the 331
  at y < 0.1**. Per cell the agreement is ≤ **0.55 %** only for
  0.15 ≤ y ≤ 0.7, ≤ **0.77 %** to y ≤ 0.8 and ≤ **2.0 %** to y ≤ 0.9; below
  y = 0.15 there is **no** agreement statement. Two corners, two mechanisms:
  at y = 0.985 they differ by **59 %** because `z_s → 0` puts the elastic
  vertex at Q′² → 0 where the form factor is 1 and the s-peak beats `Y₊`; at
  y → 0 the **uncancelled soft radiator** wins, `1 − z_s = y(1−x_A)/(1−x_A y)
  → 0` so `D(z_s) ∝ 1/y` (0.082 / 1.36 / 4.39 / 11.5 at x = 0.01 / 0.10 /
  0.30 / 0.72 along Q² = 23.88, against ratios 1.0014 / 1.061 / 3.98 / 2244),
  while the t-peak's own vertex is stuck above `t_min = M_A²x_A²/(1−x_A) ∝ x²`
  — and the Q² ≥ 20 cut forces low y to mean **high x**. The low-y corner is a
  **breakdown of the upper edge**, not evidence that the t-peak is low by
  ×6444; its excess is entirely quasi-elastic (⁶Li's coherent form factor is
  dead at Q′² ≈ Q²), both tails there are ≤ 3.2e−04 of the Born, and the cost
  is paid in the **tensor fraction of the tail**, which collapses ×1.55e−04 at
  that cell while the absolute tensor term does not move.
  The t-peak is **low by a factor 4.36** at the HERMES
  deuteron point *on the leading-log estimate*, and **by 2.36** on the exact
  tail (§ below): the upper edge **overshoots by 1.85×** there. At the low-Q²
  corner of the generator window (x = 0.01,
  y = 0.1, Q² ≈ 4 GeV²) the quasi-elastic s+p is **3.35×** the t-peak
  unsuppressed and **7.09×** at the shipped Pauli `k_F` (the s-peak **alone** is
  2.33× / 4.94×). The 10 % gate the design asked for
  against [MT69] was replaced by that construction rather than written against a
  formula transcribed from memory, and **[MT69] is still not in this tree**:
  the exact tail below is checked POLRAD-internally and against the leading log,
  never against an external exact tail.
- `RcTailModel::PolradFull` — POLRAD Eq. (18) + Appendix B + Eq. (A.4), the
  full non-peaking τ_A integration — **threw from 2026-09-02 to 2026-09-06 and
  is implemented now**, opt-in as `--rc-tail-model polrad-full`. It carries all
  **three** peaks in one quadrature **with the s-/p-peaks' own Eq. (A.4) tensor
  content**, so the sentence *"POLRAD supplies no tensor s/p peak"* — true of
  Eq. (38) — is **false of the paper**, and `--rc-sp-tensor-scale` is refused
  on it because the term it stands in for **ran**. **IT IS NOT INSIDE THE
  t-PEAK BAND**: over the sampler's 3051 accepted cells it lies between the two
  edges on **1725 (56.5 %)** and outside on **1326 (43.5 %)**, covers a median
  **0.6555** of the gap **over the 3027 cells whose gap is nonzero** (0.6620
  over all 3051 — on the other 24, `t-peak+ll` equals `t-peak` exactly and the
  fraction is undefined), and the **ratio of the σ-weighted mean shifts** is
  **0.428** — a ratio of means, **not** a σ-weighted mean of the per-cell
  fractions, which is **6.483**; and in the Q² ≥ 20, y ≤ 0.9
  window it is **above both** (8.815031e−03, +1.09 % on the t-peak, at the
  default fill P_z = 0.7). Whole-run mean `rc_tail` (200 k, seed 1234) **at
  P_z = 0.7**: **1.021778527 → 1.040274188 → 1.029702912**
  for t-peak / t-peak+ll / polrad-full; at **P_z = 0** (the plan both test
  suites use) **1.021836305 → 1.040368933 → 1.029775347** on the same build —
  the cell-σ-weighted estimator is P_z-free, the event-weighted one is not
  (re-measured 2026-09-15). Five transcription defects in
  polrad2t.tex's **Appendix B** were found and settled against the FORTRAN by
  the x_A → 0 gate (`docs/open_items/run_2026-09-06/phase_B_numbers.md` §B2.2).
  What it does **not** close: the **quasi-elastic** tensor tail, which is zero
  on all three models because Eq. (A.5) has `Im₅…₈ ≡ 0` for a spin-½ target —
  and that is the column carrying ×202 and ×7123 of the t-peak at x = 0.10 and
  0.30.
- The **polarised** quasi-elastic tail is not priced at all, and at x = 0.30 the
  tail is 99.9 % quasi-elastic.
- **No RC calculation exists for a tagged tensor asymmetry**, and none for any
  φ-dependent tensor observable at any axis. The band is nevertheless applied on
  the three tagged channels, as an explicitly UNCITED extrapolation; on the
  coherent channel it is refused outright (§8).
- Everything the band is calibrated on is **DEUTERON**. Whether the deuteron's
  *fractional* RC transfers to ⁶Li is untested and is the largest unquantified
  assumption in §8 — on top of the source itself having zero INSPIRE citations
  and being a correction magnitude rather than a measured residual.
- The ⁶Li elastic form-factor parameters are **unfitted starting values**: no
  elastic-scattering data is in this repository in machine-readable form, and the
  magnetic shape cannot be fitted at all because its source is figures only.
  σ^el_T is *quadratic* in F_q, so its band must be run and never rescaled.
- The tagged band is **clamped** (`band_tau_max` = 1) where the M-dependent
  spectator density has nodes; the clamp is a choice, the clipped fraction is
  reported, and a band reformulated on the M-averaged density is the right
  long-run answer.
- The whole family **requires an unpolarised beam** and throws otherwise, so on
  ⁷Li — whose only CLI plan is `helicity-flip` — it is reached only with
  `--pe 0` (§8).

**Coherent channel**

- Everything here is a SCENARIO with bands, "to be replaced by a real diffractive
  model"; no published calculation exists for any polarized A > 2 nucleus. The
  deformation term is scaled — with a sign flip — from a deuteron CGC
  calculation, and the flat term from bounds the repo does not cite. **One of the
  four scenario numbers has since been pinned from outside**: `slope_b` = 50 ± 10
  is reproduced by an unpolarized coherent calculation at this beam energy and is
  now citable (§9). The other three are not. In particular **`f0` cannot be
  measured that way at all** — an exclusive vector-meson generator has no
  diffractive continuum, and the fraction it *can* form inside this channel's own
  M_X window is a lower bound 40× below the default — and `amp`, `eps_b0` and the
  whole cos 2φ tensor coefficient are untouched by it, because it is unpolarized.
- **The cos 2φ tensor coefficient has not been computed for ⁶Li by a dipole-model
  run.** §10 now produces the nucleon configurations such a run would average
  over, and their zero-parameter geometric estimate agrees with `eps_b0` in sign
  and order — but **nothing in this repository has been through
  `subnucleondiffraction` or any other Good–Walker code**, so `eps_b0` and `amp`
  remain scenario values, and the sampler's own α–d wave function overshoots the
  measured asymptotic D/S ratio by about 2×.
  **"Agrees in sign and order" is as far as it goes, and since 2026-09-04 the
  gap is a number** (open item O4, `phase_C_numbers.md` §C4): inverting the map
  (`quadrupole_from_a2_slope`) on the shipped `eps_b0` = −0.08 at `slope_b` = 50
  gives an implied Q_charge(⁶Li) = **−0.9345 fm²**, **11.42×** the measured
  −0.0818 fm² and 1.52× even the α+d model's −0.615, with
  a₂(±1, |t| = 0.3) = **+0.300** — larger in magnitude than the **deuteron's**
  own digitized −0.28, for a nucleus whose quadrupole is 3.5× smaller. The
  honest ⁶Li band at that B is **−(0.0070 … 0.0527)**, i.e. §10's factor-7.5
  quadrupole budget; the old documented band −(0.04 … 0.13) is a **deuteron**
  band (the deuteron's own +0.105 at its own B_d = 33.1 GeV⁻², opposite sign)
  and contains only the α+d model row. The default is deliberately **not**
  changed — it is a reference gate — so **every generated coherent tensor
  number is 11.4× the measured-quadrupole expectation**. What that no longer
  implies is the |t| ceiling: since 2026-09-04 `COHERENT_T_MAX_DEFAULT` = 0.2
  is justified by the **anchor range** (|t| ≤ 0.30, which does not move with
  `eps_b0`), and the positivity edge is the contingent second reason, derived
  by `CoherentScenario::t_positivity_edge` — |t| = 0.245 GeV² at the shipped
  `eps_b0` and 2.80 GeV² at the measured quadrupole (three figures: that is
  arithmetic on the ROUNDED `eps_b0` = −0.0070, and the derived −0.0070024
  gives 2.7990 — "2.8000" is a digit the basis does not carry), i.e.
  positivity stops binding the moment `eps_b0` is corrected. **`eps_b0` and `slope_b` are not independent**:
  the physics is the product δ = `eps_b0`·B, so a `slope_b` scan over {40, 60}
  at fixed `eps_b0` moves a₂ by ±20 % for no physical reason — band δ, never
  `eps_b0` alone.
- The eSTARlight baseline of §9 uses one spherically symmetric Gaussian density
  per nucleus: **no α + d clustering, no diffractive minimum and no secondary
  lobes at any A**. It is a rate and slope baseline and must never be used as an
  imaging one.
- The M_X < 1.2 GeV window (ρ/ω/φ exclusive vector-meson production) carries no
  rate at all: it is a separate process deferred to Phase 2.
- `coherent_xpom` and `coherent_weighted_azimuth` have no CLI or config-file
  key. **`coherent_t_max` gained `--coherent-t-max` on 2026-09-04** (D5,
  `python/lipolgen/cli.py:633` `--coherent-t-max`) and is recorded in the npz
  `meta` with the rest of the scenario — this bullet still said it had no key
  until 2026-09-05, three hundred lines after the configuration-surface row
  had been corrected; the citation still pointed at the pre-2026-09-05 line
  until it was re-anchored and given its flag name, so the gate can see it
  drift next time. `x_coh` and ε_B0 are reachable only through the config
  file's `coherent` mapping, whose keys are the FIELD NAMES `x_coh` and
  `eps_b0` (§9). Since 2026-09-04 **ΔB is defined** — once, at that
  declaration: |F_m|² = exp(−|t|[B + ΔB_m cos 2(Φ−Φ_S)]) with ΔB_m = δ_m/2, so
  a₂(m) = −(ΔB_m/2)|t| and `eps_b0` = δ_{±1}/B = 2ΔB_{±1}/B = **−**ΔB₀/B. The
  pre-2026-09-04 label "ΔB₀/B" was off by a **sign**, not by a factor 2; the
  code was right. `CoherentScenario::delta_b_m(m)` and `slope_at_azimuth(φ, m)`
  are the only places it is computed.
- `--coherent-t2`, `--pom-set` and `--pom-rescale` are silently ignored without
  `--hadronize`.

**T2 hadronization**

- Lepton QED radiation is off (`PDF:lepton`, `TimeShower:QEDshowerByL`,
  `SpaceShower:QEDshowerByQ`); PYTHIA's own documentation states that QED
  radiation off the lepton is not implemented together with `dipoleRecoil`, so
  this is a PYTHIA limitation as well as a choice. Radiative corrections exist in
  the generator only as the opt-in **tensor-sector weights** of §8 — never as a
  momentum shift, and never applied to the T2 final state.
- No nuclear effects on the hadronic final state: no shadowing, no EMC
  modification of fragmentation, no nuclear transparency, no FSI moving any
  produced hadron, no formation-time physics. Nuclear PDFs are not used because
  PYTHIA's are hard-process-only and give an average nucleon — the opposite of
  what a tagged generator needs.
- The remnant of an off-shell nucleon is built from an **on-shell PDG-mass
  surrogate**: the off-shellness enters W²/Q² exactly, but the remnant's internal
  composition, primordial k_T and diquark mass are those of a free nucleon.
- Spin in the hard process is helicity-averaged; SPINUP is carried through the
  LHA record and consumed by nothing. MPI is absent by construction with a lepton
  beam. Charm and bottom are always massless, collinear INCOMING partons.
- Heavy flavours are never produced on the Pomeron flavour-fallback branch (all
  three H1 2006 grids — Fit A NLO, Fit B NLO and Fit B LO, one `PomH1FitAB`
  class between them — have no charm/bottom support at any (β,Q²));
  `Diffraction:doHard` is not usable, so the id-990 beam is a workaround, not
  that mechanism.
- The `StruckCluster` hook is DEPRECATED and unreachable from a `Pipeline` at the
  default tier; taking it anyway does not conserve the whole record.
- `CoherentT2::Off` leaves coherent events at T0 with no hadron-level final state.
- The `abconv` → `npsim` → EICrecon smoke test is not run from this repository;
  the HepMC3 spin attributes are a proposed convention, not an ePIC-MC-group
  standard, and `abconv`/`npsim` do not carry event attributes through to edm4hep.

**Output and CLI**

- Only HepMC3 Asciiv3 is implemented; `HepMC2Ascii` throws.
- `--lumi` alone is rejected (see §12); `--hfs-npz` requires `--hadronize`;
  `--hadronize` forces `--nthreads 1`.
- A tagged or coherent channel IMPLIES its beam species: `PipelineConfig::validate`
  throws on a mismatch, and `make_config` silently overrides the `--isotope`
  argument with the channel's own species, so `--isotope d --channel coherent`
  runs ⁶Li (§12). Only the *run-plan spin* vs ion spin check is skipped on the
  inclusive path (above); the species rule holds everywhere.
- T2 runs only when `PipelineConfig::hadronizer` is set: the tier is a hook, not
  a flag, and nothing but `--hadronize` (or a C++/Python caller) fills it.
- The four C++ example generators (`generate_inclusive`, `generate_tagged`,
  `generate_coherent`, `generate_full`) are **not** `lipolgen-run`: their flag
  spellings differ (`--slope`, `--f0`, `--eps-b0`, `--tmax`, `--pd`, `--beta`,
  `--threads`, `--out`), they take no config file and no `--hepmc`/`--npz`
  (§12, `docs/USAGE.md:1501-1509`).
- `tier`, `poisson`, `n_sigma`, `pot_config`, `with_virtual_photon`,
  `apply_optics_lumi_fraction`, `scenario` and `grid` are `make_config` keyword
  arguments only, and `kernel`, `breakup`, `struck.delta_func`/`with_perp` only
  attributes of a `PipelineConfig` object — none of them has a command-line
  switch. `struck.scenario` and `struck.grid` are attributes too, but DEAD on
  the pipeline path: the constructor overwrites both with
  `PipelineConfig::scenario`/`grid` (§12).

---

## 14. References

Keys match the `[key]` labels in the tables above. Every entry is one the
repository itself carries; where a number has **no** citation in the repository,
the row that uses it says so rather than pointing at an invented source.

1. **[HJM89]** P. Hoodbhoy, R. L. Jaffe, A. Manohar, *Novel effects in deep inelastic scattering from spin-one hadrons*, Nucl. Phys. B **312** (1989) 571. (No arXiv; pre-1991.)
2. **[JM89]** R. L. Jaffe, A. Manohar, *Deep inelastic scattering from arbitrary spin targets*, Nucl. Phys. B **321** (1989) 343. INSPIRE recid 266865.
3. **[Varsh]** D. A. Varshalovich et al., conventions for the Wigner-d and Clebsch–Gordan functions — named at `include/lipolgen/spin.hpp:24`; **no bibliographic identifier exists in the repository**.
4. **[Cosyn25]** W. Cosyn, M. C. Roldán Tomei, X. Sosa, S. Zec, Eur. Phys. J. A **61** (2025) 83, arXiv:2410.12764.
5. **[HERMES05]** HERMES Collaboration (A. Airapetian et al.), Phys. Rev. Lett. **95** (2005) 242001, arXiv:hep-ex/0506018.
6. **[POLRAD]** I. Akushevich, A. Ilyichev, N. Shumeiko, A. Soroko, A. Tolkachev, *POLRAD 2.0*, Comput. Phys. Commun. **104** (1997) 201, arXiv:hep-ph/9706516.
7. **[E143]** E143 Collaboration (K. Abe et al.), Phys. Rev. D **58** (1998) 112003.
8. **[R1998]** K. Abe et al., Phys. Lett. B **452** (1999) 194, arXiv:hep-ex/9808028 (the R1998 world fit, Eq. (2) and Table II).
9. **[WW]** The Wandzura–Wilczek relation g₂ = −g₁ + ∫ₓ¹ du g₁(u)/u — carried **by name only** in the repository (`include/lipolgen/sf.hpp:152-158`); no journal or arXiv identifier appears anywhere in it.
10. **[Fu22]** D. Fu, B.-D. Sun, Y. Dong, Phys. Rev. D **106** (2022) 116012, arXiv:2209.12161.
11. **[CWappD]** W. Cosyn, C. Weiss, arXiv:2603.23699 (2026), Appendix D — spin-j density matrix as l = 0…2j multipoles.
12. **[Miller14]** G. A. Miller, Phys. Rev. C **89** (2014) 045203, Fig. 5 (deuteron b₁, pion + hidden colour).
13. **[CDKS17]** W. Cosyn, Y.-B. Dong, S. Kumano, M. Sargsian, Phys. Rev. D **95** (2017) 074036, arXiv:1702.05337.
14. **[CK90]** F. E. Close, S. Kumano, Phys. Rev. D **42** (1990) 2377 (the sum rule ∫b₁ dx = 0).
15. **[SS90]** E. Sather, C. Schmidt, Phys. Rev. D **42** (1990) 1424 (the bag-model coefficient `C_BAG`).
16. **[CBT06]** I. C. Cloët, W. Bentz, A. W. Thomas, Phys. Lett. B **642** (2006) 210, arXiv:nucl-th/0605061, Fig. 6 (⁷Li polarized EMC at Q² = 5).
17. **[TMT18]** S. Tronchin, H. H. Matevosyan, A. W. Thomas, Phys. Lett. B **783** (2018) 247, Fig. 4 (nuclear matter, Q² = 10).
18. **[EPPS21]** K. J. Eskola, P. Paakkinen, H. Paukkunen, C. A. Salgado, Eur. Phys. J. C **82** (2022) 413 (set `EPPS21nlo_CT18Anlo_Li6`).
19. **[CT18]** T.-J. Hou et al., Phys. Rev. D **103** (2021) 014013 (`CT18NLO`, `CT18ANLO`).
20. **[NNPDFpol11]** E. R. Nocera et al. (NNPDF), Nucl. Phys. B **887** (2014) 276 (`NNPDFpol11_100`).
21. **[LHAPDF6]** A. Buckley et al., Eur. Phys. J. C **75** (2015) 132.
22. **[Wiringa14]** R. B. Wiringa, R. Schiavilla, S. C. Pieper, J. Carlson, Phys. Rev. C **89** (2014) 024305, arXiv:1309.3794, Table I (VMC AV18+UX polarizations; the 0.866/−0.037 used for ⁷Li are the higher-precision Argonne online-table values quoted by JLab PR12-14-001).
23. **[Bissey02]** F. Bissey, Phys. Rev. C **65** (2002) 064317 (³He per-nucleon P_p = −0.028, P_n = 0.86; the triton target is its p ↔ n mirror).
24. **[Schell93]** N. C. Schellingerhout, Phys. Rev. C **48** (1993) 2714 — named beside the ⁶Li cluster product at `src/core/beams.cpp:89`, but the α–d D-state probability itself is a scenario value, not a result read from that paper.
25. **[Pudliner97]** B. S. Pudliner, V. R. Pandharipande, J. Carlson, S. C. Pieper, R. B. Wiringa, Phys. Rev. C **56** (1997) 1720, arXiv:nucl-th/9705009.
26. **[Forest96]** J. L. Forest, V. R. Pandharipande, S. C. Pieper, R. B. Wiringa, R. Schiavilla, A. Arriaga, Phys. Rev. C **54** (1996) 646 (the cluster-overlap method and smoothed fits).
27. **[AV18]** R. B. Wiringa, V. G. J. Stoks, R. Schiavilla, Phys. Rev. C **51** (1995) 38 — the Hamiltonian behind every ANL VMC table used here; not cited with a specific reference in the repository.
28. **[Hulthen42]** L. Hulthén, Ark. Mat. Astron. Fys. **28B** No. 5 (1942); Hulthén & Sugawara, Handbuch der Physik **39** (1957) 1 — the analytic radial form is used by name; the repository carries no citation for it.
29. **[CS96]** C. Ciofi degli Atti, S. Simula, Phys. Rev. C **53** (1996) 1689 (n₀/n₁ spectral function, Eqs. (28), (74)–(76), Tables A.1/A.3).
30. **[CW20]** W. Cosyn, C. Weiss, Phys. Rev. C **102** (2020) 065204, arXiv:2006.03033.
31. **[CW26]** W. Cosyn, C. Weiss, arXiv:2603.23700 (2026) — polarized tagged DIS in the impulse approximation; §VI C lists the spin dependence of FSI as an open question.
32. **[BeAGLE22]** W. Chang, E.-C. Aschenauer, M. D. Baker, A. Jentsch, J.-H. Lee, Z. Tu, Z. Yin, L. Zheng, Phys. Rev. D **106** (2022) 012007, arXiv:2204.11998.
33. **[Glauber59]** R. J. Glauber, *High-Energy Collision Theory*, in Lectures in Theoretical Physics Vol. 1 (Interscience, 1959) p. 315 — the eikonal formalism, used by name.
34. **[CK11]** C. Ciofi degli Atti, L. P. Kaptari, Phys. Rev. C **83** (2011) 044602, arXiv:1011.5960, Eqs. (7)–(8) (the cluster-spectator Glauber survival product).
35. **[CK04]** C. Ciofi degli Atti, L. P. Kaptari, arXiv:nucl-th/0407024 (GEA vs plain Glauber agree to < 3–4 % for p_m < 0.6 GeV/c).
36. **[CS17]** W. Cosyn, M. Sargsian, Int. J. Mod. Phys. E **26** (2017) 1730004, arXiv:1704.06117, Eq. (11) (f_XN = σ_tot(i+ε)e^{Bt/2}, ε = −0.5, B ≈ 6 GeV⁻²; the Deeps σ(W) fit).
37. **[SW18]** M. Strikman, C. Weiss, Phys. Rev. C **97** (2018) 035209, arXiv:1706.02244 (S[IA] + S[FSI] + S[FSI²] and its unitarity sum rule).
38. **[Blinov01]** A. V. Blinov et al., Phys. At. Nucl. **64** (2001) 907, arXiv:nucl-ex/9910012 (measured α–p σ_tot = 121.5 mb, σ_el = 31.4 mb, B = 31 ± 1 GeV⁻²).
39. **[Mant24]** H. Mäntysaari, F. Salazar, B. Schenke, C. Shen, W. Zhao, Phys. Lett. B **858** (2024) 139053, arXiv:2408.13213, Eq. (9) and Fig. 4 at x_P = 1.7e-3.
40. **[TUNL6]** D. R. Tilley et al., Nucl. Phys. A **708** (2002) 3 (TUNL A = 6 evaluation — the ⁶Li breakup thresholds).
41. **[YR]** EIC Yellow Report, R. Abdul Khalek et al., Nucl. Phys. A **1026** (2022) 122447, arXiv:2103.05419 — far-forward detector matrix (Table I), hadron-beam divergences (Table 10.1), γ-matching anchor (Table 10.2). The repository names the tables but carries no bibliographic identifier for the report itself.
42. **[EPIOS]** G. Atoian et al., EPIOS white paper, arXiv:2510.10794 (lithium rigidity values and the γ synchronization windows).
43. **[AME2020]** The AME2020 atomic-mass evaluation, source of the nuclear masses tabulated in `include/lipolgen/beams.hpp:25-41`; named in the repository without a bibliographic reference.
44. **[PYTHIA83]** C. Bierlich et al., *A comprehensive guide to the physics and usage of PYTHIA 8.3*, SciPost Phys. Codebases **8** (2022), arXiv:2203.11601. Version in use: 8.317.
45. **[HepMC3]** A. Buckley et al., Comput. Phys. Commun. **260** (2021) 107310, arXiv:1912.08005.
46. **[H1FitB]** H1 Collaboration (A. Aktas et al.), Eur. Phys. J. C **48** (2006) 715, arXiv:hep-ex/0606004 — the H1 2006 Fit B diffractive PDF, PYTHIA's `PDF:PomSet = 6`. The repository names the fit but registers no bibliographic entry for it.
47. **[NumPy]** NumPy's pairwise summation and `np.interp`, reproduced from the library source (`numpy/core/src/umath/loops_utils.h.src`, `pairwise_sum_@TYPE@`, block size 128) — a source-code convention, not a publication.
48. **[Bacchetta07]** A. Bacchetta, M. Diehl, K. Goeke, A. Metz, P. J. Mulders, M. Schlegel, JHEP **02** (2007) 093 — the φ_S azimuth convention; cited in `include/lipolgen/generator.hpp:22-28` only, and not registered in the repository's literature list.
49. **[GS04]** G. I. Gakh, O. Shekhovtsova, *Radiative corrections to deep inelastic scattering of unpolarized electrons on a tensor-polarized deuteron*, JETP **99** (2004) 898, arXiv:hep-ph/0403262 — the only dedicated analytic tensor-DIS RC calculation, and the source of the 10–30 % band half-width `RC_DELTA_LOW_X`. **Zero INSPIRE citations**: never independently checked. Its two numbers are **the two ends of one panel's x window at a single Q² = 0.1** (|δ| = 0.1133 at x = 0.00966, 0.2662 at x = 0.00226), scoped by the paper to x and not to Q² — **not** a Q² spread and **not** two independent edges (corrected 2026-09-04) — and they are a *correction magnitude*, not a measured residual. `RC_DELTA_LOW_X` = 0.30 is that panel's **lowest-x** value carried **upward** in x to `RC_X_LOW` = 0.01, which lies above the panel's own top x = 0.00966 (where the paper reads 0.113): a different basis from the "conservative end of a Q² spread" this list asserted until then. Measured in `docs/open_items/run_2026-09-03/phase_B_numbers.md` §B6.3.
50. **[MT69]** L. W. Mo, Y.-S. Tsai, Rev. Mod. Phys. **41** (1969) 205 (the exact elastic radiative tail, App. B). Not on arXiv and **not obtained for this run** — named in `tests/test_rc.cpp` as the gate that was deliberately not written (§13).
51. **[dFW66]** T. de Forest, J. D. Walecka — the Fermi-gas Pauli-suppression factor S(q) of the quasi-elastic tail, as POLRAD's `ffquas` codes it. Carried in the repository **by name only**; no journal or arXiv identifier appears anywhere in it.
52. **[Moniz71]** E. J. Moniz, I. Sick, R. R. Whitney, J. R. Ficenec, R. D. Kephart, W. P. Trower, Phys. Rev. Lett. **26** (1971) 445 — the quasi-elastic e–A scaling fit that supplies k_F(⁶Li) = 0.169 GeV (`RC_QE_KF_GEV`).
53. **[eSTARlight]** M. Lomnitz, S. R. Klein, Phys. Rev. C **99** (2019) 015203 — the eSTARlight electroproduction generator used for the unpolarized coherent baseline of §9, and the place its absolute ep/eA benchmarks are published.
54. **[Chang26]** W. Chang et al., Phys. Rev. D **113** (2026) 032018, arXiv:2511.05638 — coherent J/ψ on ⁷Li at the EIC. Used as the *configuration* cross-check of §9; **it publishes no absolute cross section**, so it cannot validate one.
55. **[GK99]** E. A. George, L. D. Knutson, Phys. Rev. C **59** (1999) 598 — the measured asymptotic D/S ratio of the ⁶Li → α + d overlap, η = −0.025 ± 0.006 ± 0.010, from d + α elastic tensor analysing powers. The §10 wave functions give ≈ −0.05, about 2× that.
56. **[Pastore13]** S. Pastore, S. C. Pieper, R. Schiavilla, R. B. Wiringa, Phys. Rev. C **87** (2013) 035503 — GFMC AV18+IL7, whose Q(⁶Li) = −0.20(6) fm² sits against the measured −0.0818 fm²; the reason §10's quadrupole is a dial with a stated band rather than a model output.
57. **[MSTW08]** A. D. Martin, W. J. Stirling, R. S. Thorne, G. Watt, *Parton distributions for the LHC*, Eur. Phys. J. C **63** (2009) 189, arXiv:0901.0002 — MSTW2008 LO, the PDF CDKS computed their b₁ with, read here from PYTHIA's own `mstw2008lo.00.dat` grid through `Pythia8::MSTWpdf`.
58. **[CDBonn01]** R. Machleidt, *High-precision, charge-dependent Bonn nucleon-nucleon potential*, Phys. Rev. C **63** (2001) 024001, arXiv:nucl-th/0006014 — Appendix D and Table XX (LaTeX label `tab_dwpar`) give the analytic deuteron wave function transcribed in `cluster.hpp`; Table XV (`tab_deu`) its published B_d, A_S, η, P_D and Q_d.
